\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}d^{m}(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)\min\bigg(\exp\bigg(T-\frac{1}{\lambda}d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)\bigg),1\bigg)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{m}\min\bigg(\exp\bigg(T-\frac{1}{\lambda}n\bigg),1\bigg){\left|\{\alpha:d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)=n\}\right|}.

(47)

We next upper bound
{|\{\alpha:d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)=n\}|}
— note that

\displaystyle{|\{\alpha:d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)=n\}|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:d(x,S)=n\}|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|S|}{|\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:d(x,0)=n\}|},

(48)

where in (1) we have used the fact that any one site
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
can be in at most
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
sets
\tilde{S}_{\alpha}
(since otherwise there would be more than
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
sets
\tilde{S}_{\alpha}
that would intersect each other), and in (2) we have simply upper bounded the number of sites at a distance of
n
from
S
by the number of sites at a distance of
n
from any site in
S
.

A bound on
{|\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:d(x,0)=n\}|}
can be obtained by noting that, since
d(x,y)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}
is the Manhattan distance on the lattice,

\displaystyle{|\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:d(x,0)=n\}|}

\displaystyle={\left|\bigg\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\sum_{i=1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}=n\bigg\}\right|}\leq 2^{d}{\left|\bigg\{x\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:\sum_{i=1}^{d}x_{i}=n\bigg\}\right|},

\displaystyle=2^{d}{n+d-1\choose d-1}\leq\frac{2^{d}}{(d-1)!}(n+d-1)^{d-1}.

(49)

Combining Eqs.
48
and
49
, we obtain that

{|\{\alpha:d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)=n\}|}\leq\frac{2^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|S|}}{(d-1)!}(n+d-1)^{d-1}.

Returning to Eq.
47
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}d^{m}(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)\min\bigg(\exp\bigg(T-\frac{1}{\lambda}d(\tilde{S}_{\alpha},S)\bigg),1\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|S|}}{(d-1)!}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\min\bigg(\exp\bigg(T-\frac{1}{\lambda}n\bigg),1\bigg)n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1},

(50)

Now, we note that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\min\bigg(\exp\bigg(T-\frac{n}{\lambda}\bigg),1\bigg)n^{m}(n+d-1)^{k}

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{n=0}^{\lceil\lambda T\rceil}n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor}^{\infty}n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1}\exp\bigg(T-\frac{n}{\lambda}\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\lceil\lambda T\rceil}n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor)^{m}(n+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor+d-1)^{d-1}\exp\bigg(T-\frac{\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor}{\lambda}-\frac{n}{\lambda}\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{n=0}^{\lceil\lambda T\rceil}n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor)^{m}(n+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor+d-1)^{d-1}\exp(-n/\lambda),

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu3\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}Y_{m}(\lceil\lambda T\rceil)+2^{d-2}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor^{(1-\sigma)m}(d-1+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor)^{(1-\sigma^{\prime})(d-1)}X_{\sigma m+\sigma^{\prime}(d-1)}(\lambda),

(51)

where for
p\in\{0,1,2\dots\}
and
m\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle X_{p}(\lambda)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{p}\exp(-n/\lambda)\text{ and }Y_{m}(z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\lceil z\rceil}n^{m}(n+d-1)^{d-1}.

In (1), we have split the summation over
n
into two summation — one summation involving only
n\leq\lceil\lambda T\rceil
, where we have used
\min(\exp(T-n/\lambda),1)\leq 1
, and the other summation involving
n\geq\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor
, where we have used
\min(\exp(T-n/\lambda),1)\leq\exp(T-n/\lambda)
. In (2), we have used the fact that
\lambda T\leq\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor
, and thus
\exp(T-\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor/\lambda)\leq 1
. In (3), we have used the fact that for
a,b>0
,
(a+b)^{d}\leq 2^{d-1}(a^{d}+b^{d})
. We will now pick the functions
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
from the lemma statement to be,

\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)=\frac{2^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|S|}}{(d-1)!}\bigg(Y_{m}(\lceil\lambda T\rceil)+2^{d-2}\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor^{(1-\sigma)m}(d-1+\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor)^{(1-\sigma^{\prime})(d-1)}X_{\sigma m+\sigma^{\prime}(d-1)}(\lambda)\bigg).

From Eqs.
46
,
50
and
51
, it follows that the lemma statement holds with this choice of
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
. Furthermore, since the dependence of
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
on
T
is entirely through
\lfloor\lambda T\rfloor
and
\lceil\lambda T\rceil
, it follows that it is a piecewise continuous function of
T
for a fixed
\lambda
. It is also clear that
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
is non-decreasing in
T
for a fixed
\lambda
. Finally, to understand the asymptotic behavior of
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
as
\lambda,T\to\infty
, we note that
X_{p}(\lambda)\leq O(\lambda^{p+1})
and
Y_{m}(\lceil\lambda T\rceil)\leq O((\lambda T)^{m+d})
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)

\displaystyle\leq O((\lambda T)^{m+d})+\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}O((\lambda T)^{(1-\sigma)m+(1-\sigma^{\prime})(d-1)})O(\lambda^{(\sigma m+\sigma^{\prime}(d-1)+1)}),

\displaystyle\leq O((\lambda T)^{m+d})+\sum_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}}O((\lambda T)^{(1-\sigma)m+(1-\sigma^{\prime})(d-1)})O((\lambda T)^{(\sigma m+\sigma^{\prime}(d-1)+1)}),

\displaystyle\leq O((\lambda T)^{m+d}).

Furthermore, for
T=0
,

\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,0)=\frac{2^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}{|S|}}{(d-1)!}\bigg(Y_{m}(0)+2^{d-2}(d-1)^{d-1}X_{m}(\lambda)+2^{d-2}X_{m+d-1}(\lambda)\bigg)\leq O(\lambda^{m+d})\text{ as }\lambda\to\infty.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

B.1
Proof of Lemma
5

In this appendix, we will establish Lemma
5
. We first establish the following

Lemma 13
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Then for
q_{\alpha}(s),\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s)
, with
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(2)}
, as defined in Lemma
1
,

{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha}(s))\right|}\leq 2\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg)\text{ for any }\alpha,

and

{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}\leq 4\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg)\text{ for any }\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},j.

Proof.

Using the Lieb-Robinson bounds (Lemma
3
) together with the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})|}

\displaystyle={|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}(\rho(0)))|}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}(O(\tau))|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\rho(0)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq 2\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg).

Next, consider
\text{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s))
. For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
we obtain that

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}((\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s))]])\right|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega}{\left\|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}((\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq{\left\|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]]\right\|},

where, in the last step, we have used Lemma
2
. Next, we can use Lemma
4
together with the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty},{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(\pm)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
to obtain

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(1)}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(s))|}\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

Next, for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
, we obtain that,

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\alpha,{\alpha^{\prime}}}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\rho_{\omega}(s)]])\right|},

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]L_{\alpha}]|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{A}(\rho_{\omega}(s))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\left(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O}))\right),1\right).

Next, we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})|}
. For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}\left([L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\omega}(s))]\right))|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}(O(\tau))]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\omega}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}(O(\tau))]\right\|},

where, in the last step, we have used Lemma
2
. Next, we use Lemma
4
together with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
and
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
to obtain

{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

Next we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}_{1}
. For
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u^{\prime})},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u}^{\prime})}\rho_{\omega}(s))]])|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{({u}^{\prime})},[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},O(\tau)]]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u}^{\prime})}\rho_{\omega}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{({u}^{\prime})},[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},O(\tau)]]\right\|},

where in the last step we have used Lemma
2
. Furthermore, from Lemma
4
and the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
it follows that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

(52)

Similarly, for
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega^{2}}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})(\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(n_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s))))\right|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega^{2}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}\big(O(\tau)\big)|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(n_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}\big(O(\tau)\big)|\hskip-1.084pt|},

where, again, in the last step, we have used Lemma
2
. Next, we can use Lemma
3
together with the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 4
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{a}d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})\bigg),1\bigg).

(53)

Eqs.
53
and
52
together establish establish a bound on
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}
for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
and is consistent with the lemma statement.
∎

Lemma
5
, repeated
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Then for
q_{\alpha}
as defined in Lemma
1
, then there is a non-decreasing piecewise continuous function
\nu
such that
\nu(t)\leq O(t^{d})
as
t\to\infty
and

\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})\right|}\leq\nu(\tau),

and for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s))\right|}\leq\nu^{2}(\tau),

where, for
j\in\{3,4\}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
and for
j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(j)}
where
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
.

Proof.

We will prove this lemma using Lemmas
12
and
13
. Consider first

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{|\text{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\alpha}\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg),

\displaystyle=2\xi^{(0,1)}_{a,\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq 2\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq 2e\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau),

(54)

where in the last step, we have used the fact that as per Lemma
12

\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
is a non-decreasing function of
\lambda
for a fixed
T
.
Similarly, for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}{|\text{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s)|},

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})}{2a}\bigg),1\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad=4\xi_{2a,e\eta_{S_{O}}}^{(0,2)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq 4\big(\max(e\eta_{S_{O}},1)\big)^{2}\left(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\right)^{2}\leq 4e^{2}\big(\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\big)^{2}\big(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\big)^{2}.

(55)

From Eqs.
54
and
55
, it follows that choosing
\nu(\tau)=2e\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)
satisfies the lemma statement. It can also be noted that, from the asymptotics of
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
in Lemma
12
,
\nu(\tau)\leq O(\tau^{d})
and that since
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
, for a fixed
\lambda
, is a non-decreasing and piecewise continuous function of
T
,
\nu(\tau)
is also a piecewise continuous non-decreasing function of
\tau
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
6

In this appendix, we will outline a proof of Lemma
6
. A convenient technical lemma to use for this proof is below.

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
O
is a spatially local observable and let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
be its Heisenberg-picture evolution with respect to a geometrically local Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}
of the form of Eq.
14
and with fixed point
\sigma
. Suppose that
O
obeys the local rapid mixing condition (Eq.
20
). Furthermore, suppose
\sigma_{\omega}(s)
is a time-dependent operator which satisfies
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq f(\omega)
for some
f(\omega)>0
. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha},\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
be superoperators supported on
\tilde{S}_{\alpha}
and
\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
respectively which satisfy
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}(I)=\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}(I)=0
and
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and
\forall\alpha:{|\{\alpha^{\prime}:\tilde{S}_{\alpha}\cap\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\}|}\leq\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
for some
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}>0
. Then,

(a)

For any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma),

(56)

where if
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)
, defined in Eq.
20
, is
O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
.

(b)

For any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}\leq f(\omega)\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma),

(57)

where if
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)
, defined in Eq.
20
, is
O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(d+1)(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
.

(c)

For any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}e^{-2(s-s^{\prime})/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma),

(58)

where if
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)
, defined in Eq.
20
, is
O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(\kappa+1)(2d+1)})
.

In Eqs.
56
-
58
, the functions
\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma)
,
\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma)
and
\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)
depend on
\mathcal{Z}
,
a
, and
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

(a) The idea behind this proof is similar to the example detailed in the main text i.e. to separate out the integral in the summation with into a short-time and long-time integral. The short-time integral is upper bounded using the Lieb-Robinson bounds, and the long-time integral is bounded using the local rapid-mixing property (Eq.
20
). We begin by noting that

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}={\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))ds\right|}\leq e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})+e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha}),

(59)

where

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})={\left|\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))ds\right|},

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})={\left|\int_{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))ds\right|},

and
t_{\alpha}>0
is to be chosen later. We bound
e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})
using Lieb-Robinson bounds. First we note that by Lemma
3
,

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))\right|}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}^{\dagger}_{\alpha}O(s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 2f(\omega)\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}s-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\right).

(60)

This allows us to bound
e^{\leq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})
:

\displaystyle e^{\leq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)\eta_{S_{O}}\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}s-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\bigg)ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\min(t,t_{\alpha})-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\bigg)\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}ds,

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha}-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\bigg)

(61)

We bound
e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})
using the local rapid mixing condition. Note that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))\right|}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))\right|}+{\left|\textnormal{Tr}((O(s)-\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)I)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))\right|},

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|O(s)-\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)I|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}2f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma s},

(62)

where in (1) we have the fact that for any operator
X
,
\text{Tr}(\mathcal{K_{\alpha}}(X))=\text{Tr}(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{\dagger}(I)X)=0
and in (2), we have used Eq.
20
. For
t\leq t_{\alpha}
, since
\min(t,t_{\alpha})=t
, it follows immediately that

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha})=0.

(63)

For
t\geq t_{\alpha}
, we can obtain two upper bounds on
e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha})
. The first upper bound is using Eq.
62
which yields,

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha}({t,t_{\alpha}})\leq 2f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\int_{t_{\alpha}}^{t}e^{-\gamma s}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}ds\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha}}.

(64)

Alternatively, we can also bound

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})\leq\int_{t_{\alpha}}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}{|\text{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))|}ds\leq\int_{t_{\alpha}}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)/\omega^{2}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}ds\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega).

(65)

From the bounds in Eqs.
63
,
64
and
65
, we obtain that,

e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\min\big(k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha}},1\big)\text{ for any }t,t_{\alpha}.

Combining the bounds for the short-time and long-time integrals, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}\text{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\sum_{\alpha}\bigg(\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha}-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg)+\min\big(k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha}},1)\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\sum_{\alpha}\bigg(\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha}-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg)+\min\big(\exp\big(T_{\gamma}-\gamma t_{\alpha}\big),1)\bigg),

(66)

where
T_{\gamma}=\log(\max(k({|S_{O}|},\gamma),1))
— note also that
T_{\gamma}>0
. We now pick
t_{\alpha}=d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})/(8e\mathcal{Z}a)
and apply Lemma
12
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}\text{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\big(\xi_{2a,\eta_{S_{O}}}^{(0,1)}(0)+\xi^{(0,1)}_{{8e\mathcal{Z}a\gamma^{-1}},1}(T_{\gamma})\big),

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\underbrace{\big(\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,0)+\nu^{(0)}(8e\mathcal{Z}a\gamma^{-1},T_{\gamma})\big)}_{\zeta^{(0)}(\gamma)}.

(67)

Now, if
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
T_{\gamma}\leq O(\gamma^{-\kappa})
. Therefore, as
\gamma\to 0

\zeta^{(0)}(\gamma)=\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,0)+\nu^{(0)}(8e\mathcal{Z}a\gamma^{-1},T_{\gamma})\leq O((\gamma^{-1}T_{\gamma})^{d})\leq O(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)}),

which proves part (a) of the lemma. Note that
\zeta^{(0)}(\gamma)
also depends on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
through
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
.

(b) We proceed similarly to part (a), and note that

{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\text{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s))ds\right|}={\left|\int_{0}^{t}\text{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s))ds\right|}\leq e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})+e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha}),

where

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})={\left|\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))ds\right|},

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha})={\left|\int_{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s)))ds\right|},

where
t_{\alpha}>0
is to be chosen later. We proceed similarly to part (a). To bound
e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})
, we use Eq.
60
to obtain

\displaystyle e_{\alpha}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}s-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\right)ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\min(t,t_{\alpha})-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\right)\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha})}ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)t_{\alpha}\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha}-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{a}\right),1\right).

(68)

Next we bound
e_{\alpha}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha})
. If
t\leq t_{\alpha}
, then
\min(t,t_{\alpha})=t
and we have

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})=0.

(69)

If instead
t\geq t_{\alpha}
, we can use Eq.
62
to write

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})\leq 2f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\int_{t_{\alpha}}^{t}e^{-\gamma s}ds\leq 2f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\gamma^{-1}e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha}}..

(70)

Combining Eqs.
69
and
70
, we obtain that

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})\leq 2f(\omega)\gamma^{-1}k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha}}\text{ for any }t,t_{\alpha}.

(71)

We now pick

t_{\alpha}=T^{\prime}_{\gamma}+\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})}{8e\mathcal{Z}a},

where
T^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\gamma^{-1}\max(\log(\gamma^{-1}k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)))
. We accordingly obtain the following expressions for the short-time and long-time integrals:

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}e^{\leq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq f(\omega)\left(2T^{\prime}_{\gamma}\xi^{(0,1)}_{2a,\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})+\frac{1}{4e\mathcal{Z}a}\xi^{(1,1)}_{2a,\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq 2f(\omega)\xi^{(0,1)}_{8e\mathcal{Z}a\gamma^{-1},1}(0),

(72)

where we have used
\xi^{(m,k)}_{\lambda,x}(t)
from Lemma
12
. Using Lemma
12
we obtain that,

\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(\sigma_{\omega}(s)))ds\right|}\leq\sum_{\alpha}e^{\leq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})+e^{\geq}_{\alpha}(t,t_{\alpha})\leq f(\omega)\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma),

where

\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma)=\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\left(2T^{\prime}_{\gamma}\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})+\frac{1}{4e\mathcal{Z}}\nu^{(1)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})+2\nu^{(0)}(8e\mathcal{Z}a\gamma^{-1},0)\right).

Note that
\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma)
also depends on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
through its dependence on
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
. If
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
T^{\prime}_{\gamma}\leq O(\gamma^{-(\kappa+1)})
and so, using the asymptotics of
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
from Lemma
12
, we obtain that

\zeta^{(2)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(\kappa+1)})\times O(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)})+O(\gamma^{-(d+1)(\kappa+1)})+O(\gamma^{-d})\leq O(\gamma^{-(d+1)(\kappa+1)}),

which establishes the lemma.

(c) We begin by rewriting

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}e^{-2(s-s^{\prime})/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|}={\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|},

(73)

where we have made the change of variables
s\to t-s
and
s^{\prime}\to t-s^{\prime}
. Next, we define the short-time and long-time integrals as follows:

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})+e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}),

where

\displaystyle e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})={\left|\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|},

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})={\left|\int_{\min(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|},

and
t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}>0
will be chosen later. Consider first
e_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{\leq}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})
— this can be upper bounded using the Lieb-Robinson bounds. Applying Lemma
4
and using
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
, we see that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{\left\|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{\dagger}(O(s))\right\|}{\left\|\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq 4f(\omega)\min\left(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}s-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})+d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\right),1\right),

leading to the bound on
e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})
:

\displaystyle e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq 4f(\omega)\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}\min\left(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}s-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})+d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))\right),1\right)ds^{\prime}ds,

\displaystyle\leq 4f(\omega)\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})+d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))\right),1\bigg)\int_{0}^{\min(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}ds^{\prime}ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2\omega^{2}f(\omega)t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})+d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))\right),1\bigg).

(75)

Next we consider
e_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{\geq}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})
— again, to upper bound this term, we use the local rapid mixing property (Eq.
20
). For any
0\leq s,s^{\prime}\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime}))\right|}+{\left|\textnormal{Tr}((O(s)-\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)I)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})))\right|},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|O(s)-\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)I|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(t-s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu4\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma s}.

(76)

where in (1) we have used the fact that
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(I)=0
and therefore for any
X
,
\text{Tr}(\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X))=\text{Tr}(\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\dagger}(I)X)=0
. In (2), we have used Eq.
20
. Now, for
t<t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
,
\min(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})=t
and therefore

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})=0.

(77)

Next, for
t\geq t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
, using Eq.
76
, we obtain that

\displaystyle e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq 4f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\int_{t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2(s^{\prime}-s)/\omega^{2}}e^{-\gamma s}ds^{\prime}ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2\omega^{2}f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\int_{t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}^{t}e^{-\gamma s}ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{-1}\omega^{2}f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}.

(78)

From the bounds in Eq.
77
and
78
, we obtain that

e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})\leq 2\gamma^{-1}\omega^{2}f(\omega)k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)e^{-\gamma t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}}\text{ for any }t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}.

We now pick

t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}={T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{16e\mathcal{Z}a}\bigg(d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha})+d(S_{O},\tilde{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\bigg)

where
T_{\gamma}^{\prime}=\gamma^{-1}\max\big(\log(\gamma^{-1}k({|S_{O}|},\gamma),1)\big)
. With this choice, we obtain the following expressions for the long-time and short-time integrals:

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\bigg(2T_{\gamma}^{\prime}\xi_{4a,e\eta_{S_{O}}}^{(0,2)}\big(4e\mathcal{Z}{T_{\gamma}^{\prime}}\big)+\frac{1}{8e\mathcal{Z}a}\xi_{4a,e\eta_{S_{O}}}^{(1,2)}\big(4e\mathcal{Z}T_{\gamma}^{\prime}\big)\bigg),

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq{2\omega^{2}f(\omega)}\xi^{(0,2)}_{16e\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{Z}a,1}(0),

where we have used
\xi^{m,k}_{\lambda,x}(t)
defined in Lemma
12
. Using Lemma
12
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}e^{-2(s-s^{\prime})/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})))ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\big(e^{\leq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})+e^{\geq}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t,t_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}f(\omega)\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma),

where

\displaystyle\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)=\max(e\eta_{S_{O}},1)^{2}\left(2T^{\prime}_{\gamma}\left(\nu^{(0)}(4a,4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4e\mathcal{Z}a}\nu^{(0)}(4a,4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})\nu^{(1)}(4a,4e\mathcal{Z}T^{\prime}_{\gamma})\right)+2\left(\nu^{(0)}(16e\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{Z})\right)^{2}.

Note that
\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)
also depends on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
through its dependence on
\nu^{(m)}(\gamma)
. If
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
,
T_{\gamma}\leq O(\gamma^{-(\kappa+1)})
and using the asymptotics of
\nu^{(m)}(\lambda,T)
from Lemma
12
, we obtain that

\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)\leq O\left(\gamma^{-(\kappa+1)}\right)\times O\left(\gamma^{-2d(\kappa+1)}\right)+O\left(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)}\right)\times O\left(\gamma^{-(d+1)(\kappa+1)}\right)+O\left(\gamma^{-2d}\right)\leq O\left(\gamma^{-(2d+1)(\kappa+1)}\right),

which establishes the lemma.
∎

Lemma
6
, repeated
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Furthermore, suppose
O
is rapidly mixing with respect to
\mathcal{L}
and satisfies Eq.
20
with
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
as
\gamma\to 0
. Then for
q_{\alpha}
as defined in Lemma
1
,

\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)q_{\alpha})e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}ds\right|}\leq\omega^{2}\lambda^{(1)}(\gamma),

where
\lambda^{(1)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
; and for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\lambda^{(2)}(\gamma),

where
\lambda^{(2)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(2d+1)(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
and for
j\in\{3,4\}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
, and for
j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(j)}
where
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
.

Proof.

Noting from Lemma
1
that
q_{\alpha}=\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}(\rho(0))
, and that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\rho(0)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}=1
, from the application of Lemma
14
(a), we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\text{Tr}(O(t-s)q_{\alpha})e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}ds\right|}\leq\omega^{2}\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma),

and thus we may choose
\lambda^{(1)}(\gamma)=\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma)
. By Lemma
14
, so long as
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O\left(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa})\right)
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
\zeta^{(1)}(\gamma)\leq O\left(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)}\right)
as
\gamma\to 0
.
Next, we note that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}=\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\text{Tr}\bigg({O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}\bigg(\frac{s^{\prime}}{\omega^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)}e^{-2(s-s^{\prime})/\omega^{2}}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}

(79)

We can now consider
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
separately — for
j=1
, we have from Lemma
1
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c.}
with
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},X]
,
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})=-\omega^{-1}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and that, by Lemma
2
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2
. Thus, from Eq.
79
and Lemma
14
(c), we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(80)

Similarly, for
j=2
, we have from Lemma
1
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c.}
where
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}X]
,
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})=-i\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))/2
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2
. Thus, from Eq.
79
and Lemma
14
, we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(81)

For
j=3
, we have from Lemma
1
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(3)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\sum_{u\in\{-,+\}}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{(u)}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)}(\sigma_{\omega}^{(u)}(s^{\prime}))
where
\mathcal{K}^{(u)}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}
,
\mathcal{J}^{(u)}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}^{(u)}(s^{\prime})=i\omega^{-1}\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\omega}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}^{(u)}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}^{(u)}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and, using Lemma
2
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma^{(\bar{u})}_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2
. Thus, from Eq.
79
and Lemma
14
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(3)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(82)

Finally, for
j=4
, we have from Lemma
1
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(4)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(\sigma_{\omega}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c.}
according to the following definitions: We define
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L^{\dagger}_{\alpha},X]
.
J_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(X)=L^{(u)}X
if
u^{\prime}=-
and
J_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(X)=XL^{(u)}
if
u^{\prime}=+
. Finally,
\sigma_{\omega}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(s^{\prime})=\omega^{-2}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
if
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{\omega}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(s^{\prime})=u^{\prime}\omega^{-2}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
if
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
. We note that for any
u,u^{\prime}
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u,u^{\prime})}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and, using Lemma
2
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/4
. Thus, applying Eq.
79
and Lemma
14
(c), we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(4)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq 2\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(83)

By Lemma
14
, so long as
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O\left(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa})\right)
as
\gamma\to 0
, then
\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)\leq O\left(\gamma^{-(2d+1)(\kappa+1)}\right)
as
\gamma\to 0
. Considering Eqs.
80
-
83
, we may choose
\lambda^{(2)}(\gamma)=2\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma)
to satisfy the lemma statement.
∎

Appendix C
Circuit-to-geometrically local Lindbladian encoding

In this appendix, we construct an encoding of a quantum circuit into a 2D geometrically local Lindbladian with a unique fixed point. We prove a useful result about its convergence to fixed point. Our construction follows from combining the dissipative quantum computing gadgets in Ref.
[
47
]
with the quantum circuit to Hamiltonian encoding in Ref.
[
48
]
. We remark that Ref.
[
47
]
already suggested, in its supplementary, that it should be possible to perform dissipative quantum computation with just a 2D nearest-neighbour Lindbladian but did not provide an explicit construction. We fill that gap in this appendix.

C.1
Review of the
O(\log N)
-local

As a review of the basic construction and proof technique involved in encoding a quantum circuit into the fixed point of an Lindbladian, we first analyze the 5-local construction in Ref.
[
47
]
. While Ref.
[
47
]
laid out the circuit to Lindbladian encoding and analyzed the eigenvalue spectrum of the resulting Lindbladian, they did not explicitly bound the error between the state obtained by evolution under the Lindbladian and the fixed point. This subsection also serves to fill this gap.

Figure 8:
Architecture of the circuit being encoded into the Lindbladian assumed in section
C.1
. The identity gates are treated as separate time-steps. Any poly
(N)
depth quantum circuit on
N
qubits can be brought into this architecture with at-most a
\text{poly}(N)
overhead in the circuit depth.

We will begin by first reviewing the circuit to Lindbladian encoding that is a slight modification of the one shown in Ref.
[
47
]
— consider a quantum circuit on
N
qubits and of depth
T=\text{poly}(N)
. In preparation for the analysis of the geometrically local Lindbladian, we will assume the unitaries
U_{1},U_{2}\dots U_{T}
applied in each time-step of the circuit are in the architecture as shown in Fig.
8
. We will construct a master equation on the Hilbert space
(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes N}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{T+1}
of
N
data qubits and a
(T+1)-
level system, which will be the clock qudit. We consider a Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
with two sets of jump operators,
first
,
\{S_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\{1,2\dots N\}}
encoding the initialization of the data qubits to
\ket{0}
and
second
,
\{L_{t}\}_{t\in\{1,2\dots T\}}
to encode the quantum gates:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}+\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{L_{s}},

(84a)

\displaystyle S_{\alpha}=\ket{0_{\alpha}}\!\bra{1_{\alpha}}\otimes\sum_{t=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{t}\!\bra{t}\text{ for }\alpha\in[1:N],

(84b)

\displaystyle L_{s}=U_{s}\otimes\ket{s}\!\bra{s-1}+\text{h.c.}\text{ if }s\in[1:T].

(84c)

As we will demonstrate below, the unique fixed point of this master equation is given by the state

\displaystyle\sigma=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{s=0}^{T}\ket{\phi_{s}}\!\bra{\phi_{s}}\otimes\ket{s}\!\bra{s},

(85)

where
\ket{\phi_{s}}=U_{s}U_{s-1}\dots U_{1}\ket{0}^{\otimes N}
, with
\ket{\phi_{0}}=\ket{0}^{\otimes N}
Furthermore, we will also establish an upper bound on
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}-\text{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}
to assess the time taken to converge to the fixed point. We remark that our analysis closely follows Ref.
[
47
]
, where they calculated the eigenvalues
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
. However, since the eigenvector matrix of a Lindbladian superoperator is not necessarily unitary and can be badly conditioned, just the eigenvalues are not enough bound the error
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}-\text{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}
. Below, we build on the analysis in Ref.
[
47
]
and provide a concrete bound on
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}-\text{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}
.

To proceed with our analysis, we need the following technical lemma.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose
M\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
is the tridiagonal matrix given by

M\cong\sum_{i=0}^{k-2}\big(\ket{i}\!\bra{i+1}+\ket{i+1}\!\bra{i}-\ket{i}\!\bra{i}-\ket{i+1}\!\bra{i+1}\big),

then

{|\hskip-1.084pt|\exp(Mt)-\ket{\xi}\!\bra{\xi}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\textnormal{op}}\leq\exp\bigg(-4t\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2k}\bigg)\bigg)=\exp\bigg(-\Omega\bigg(\frac{t}{k^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\text{ as }k\to\infty,

where
\ket{\xi}=k^{-1/2}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\ket{n}
. Furthermore, if
d\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a vector with only non-negative elements and with smallest non-zero element
d_{0}
then

{|\hskip-1.084pt|\exp((M-\textnormal{diag}(d))t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\textnormal{op}}\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{t}{k}\min\bigg(d_{0},4\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2k}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)=\exp\bigg(-\Omega\bigg(\frac{t}{k^{3}}\bigg)\bigg)\text{ as }k\to\infty.

Proof.

We can analytically diagonalize
M
— consider the vector
\ket{v_{n}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, for
n\in\{0,1,2\dots k-1\}
given by

\ket{v_{n}}=\bigg(\frac{2}{k}\bigg)^{1/2}\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\cos\bigg(\frac{\pi n}{k}\bigg(m+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\bigg)\ket{m},

\ket{v_{n}}
is an eigenvector of
M
with eigenvalue
\lambda_{n}=-4\sin^{2}(n\pi/2k)
. Note that
\ket{v_{0}}=\ket{\xi}
with
\lambda_{0}=0
. It then follows immediately from the diagonalization of
M
that

{|\hskip-1.084pt|\exp(Mt)-\ket{\xi}\!\bra{\xi}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\text{op}}={\left\|\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{\lambda_{m}t}\ket{v_{m}}\!\bra{v_{m}}\right\|}_{\text{op}}=\exp({\lambda_{1}t})=\exp\bigg(-4t\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2k}\bigg)\bigg)

To bound
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\exp((M-\textnormal{diag}(d))t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}
, we use Ref.
[
67
]
Lemma 14.4 which is as follows: Suppose
A_{1},A_{2}\succeq 0
are non-negative operators with the minimum positive eigenvalue being at least
c_{0}
. Suppose
\mathcal{N}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{2}
are the null spaces of
A_{1},A_{2}
respectively and
\theta=\cos^{-1}\big(\max_{\ket{n_{1}}\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}\max_{\ket{n_{2}}\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}{|\bra{n_{1}}n_{2}\rangle|}/{|\hskip-1.084pt|n_{1}|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|n_{2}|\hskip-1.084pt|}\big)
be the angle between
\mathcal{N}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{2}
, then
\lambda_{\text{min}}(A_{1}+A_{2})\geq 2c_{0}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)
. We now apply this with
A_{1}=\textnormal{diag}(d)
and
A_{2}=-M
. Note that this implies that
c_{0}=\min(d_{0},4\sin^{2}(\pi/2k))
and
\cos\theta\leq(k-1)/k
. Thus, we obtain that

\lambda_{\text{max}}(M-\textnormal{diag}(d))=-\lambda_{\text{min}}(-M+\textnormal{diag}(d))\leq-\frac{1}{k}\min\bigg(d_{0},4\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2k}\bigg)\bigg).

Now, using the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\exp((M-\textnormal{diag}(d))t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq\exp(\lambda_{\text{max}}(M-\textnormal{diag}(d))t)
, the lemma statement follows.
∎

Lemma 16

(Expanding on Ref.
[
47
]
)
.

The state
\sigma
described in Eq.
85
is the unique fixed point of the lindbladian in Eq.
84
, and for sufficiently large
T,N
,

{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}-\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq c_{0}(T,N)\exp(-a_{0}(T)t),

where

\displaystyle c_{0}(T,N)=64(T+1)^{1/2}N^{4}2^{9N/2}\text{ and }a_{0}(T)=\frac{4}{T+1}\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2(T+1)}\bigg)

Proof.

In this proof, we will work with the vectorized notation introduced in section
III
. In both vectorized and unvectorized states or operators, we will consistently write the operator acting on the space of the data qubits to the left of the tensor product
\otimes
, and the operator acting on the clock qudit on the right of the tensor product
\otimes
. Following Ref.
[
47
]
, as a first step, we introduce the unitary

V=\sum_{s=0}^{T}U_{s}U_{s-1}\dots U_{0}\otimes\ket{s}\!\bra{s},

with
U_{0}=I
. Note that
\forall\alpha\in[1:N]:

\displaystyle V^{\dagger}S_{\alpha}V

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\alpha-1}V^{\dagger}\big(\ket{0_{\alpha}}\!\bra{1_{\alpha}}\otimes\ket{t}\!\bra{t}\big)V,

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\alpha-1}U_{0}^{\dagger}U_{1}^{\dagger}\dots U_{t}^{\dagger}(\ket{0_{\alpha}}\!\bra{1_{\alpha}})U_{t}U_{t-1}\dots U_{0}\otimes\ket{t}\!\bra{t},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}\sum_{t=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{0_{\alpha}}\!\bra{1_{\alpha}}\otimes\ket{t}\!\bra{t},

(86)

where in (1), we have used the fact that the unitaries
U_{1}\dots U_{\alpha-1}
act only on qubits
1,2\dots\alpha-1
due to the assumption that the circuit is of the form shown in Fig. . Furthermore,
\forall s\in[1:T]:

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{s}

\displaystyle=V^{\dagger}L_{s}V=V^{\dagger}\big(U_{s}\otimes\ket{s}\!\bra{s-1}\big)V+\text{h.c.}=\ket{s}\!\bra{s-1}+\text{h.c.}.

(87)

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\text{ref}}:=V_{l}^{\dagger}V_{r}^{T}\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}V_{l}V_{r}^{*}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}+\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}}.

(88)

Next, we diagonalize the Lindbladian in the space of the data qubits. For this, we consider the following (non-unitary) transformation

X=\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}X_{j}X_{j+1}\dots X_{N}\otimes\Pi_{j},

where for
j=N+1
,
X_{j}X_{j+1}\dots X_{N}:=I
and

\displaystyle X_{i}=P_{i,l}^{0}P_{i,r}^{0}+\sigma_{i,l}\sigma_{i,r}-P_{i,l}^{1}P_{i,r}^{1}+P_{i,l}^{0}P_{i,r}^{1}+P_{i,l}^{1}P_{i,r}^{0},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}=\sum_{s=0}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}},\Pi_{j}=\sum_{s=0}^{j-1}\big(\ket{j_{l}s_{r}}\!\bra{j_{l}s_{r}}+\ket{s_{l}j_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}j_{r}}\big)\text{ and }\Pi_{N+1}=I-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Pi_{j},

with
P^{\alpha}_{i,\lambda}=\ket{\alpha_{i,\lambda}}\!\bra{\alpha_{i,\lambda}}
, for
\alpha\in\{0,1\},\lambda\in\{l,r\}
. It can be checked that
X_{i}
satisfies
X_{i}\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{i}}X_{i}=-\big(P_{i,r}^{1}+P_{i,l}^{1}\big)/2
and that
X_{i}=X_{i}^{-1}
(and therefore
X=X^{-1}
). Next, we compute
X\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}X
and
X\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}}X
— note that
\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}
is explicitly given by

\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}=\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\alpha}}\otimes\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(P_{\alpha,l}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}+P_{\alpha,r}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\bigg)

To compute
X\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}X
, we observe that for
\alpha\in[1:N]

\displaystyle\Pi_{j}\bigg(\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\bigg)\Pi_{j^{\prime}}=0\text{ if }j\neq j^{\prime}\text{ or }j=j^{\prime}>\alpha,

(89a)

\displaystyle\Pi_{j}\bigg(\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\bigg)\Pi_{j^{\prime}}=\Pi_{j}\bigg(\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s^{\prime}_{l}s_{r}}\!\bra{s^{\prime}_{l}s_{r}}\bigg)\Pi_{j^{\prime}}=0\text{ if }j\neq j^{\prime}\text{ or }j=j^{\prime}\leq\alpha

(89b)

Therefore, we obtain that, for all
\alpha\in[1:N]

\displaystyle X\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}X^{-1}

\displaystyle=X\bigg(\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\alpha}}\otimes\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\bigg)X-\frac{1}{2}X\bigg(P_{\alpha,l}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\bigg)X-\frac{1}{2}X\bigg(P_{\alpha,r}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\bigg)X,

\displaystyle=X_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}\otimes\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(P_{\alpha,l}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}+P_{\alpha,r}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\bigg),

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\big(P_{\alpha,r}^{(1)}+P_{\alpha,l}^{(1)}\big)\otimes\sum_{s,s^{\prime}=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}s^{\prime}_{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(P_{\alpha,l}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}\!\bra{s_{l}s_{r}^{\prime}}+P_{\alpha,r}^{1}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{s^{\prime}=\alpha}^{T}\ket{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{l}^{\prime}s_{r}}\bigg),

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(P_{\alpha,l}^{(1)}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{l}}\!\bra{s_{l}}+P_{\alpha,r}^{(1)}\otimes\sum_{s=0}^{\alpha-1}\ket{s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{r}}\bigg).

Furthermore, it can also be easily seen that for any
s\in[1:T]
and
j\in[1:N+1]
,
[\Pi_{j},\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}]}]=0
, and
\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}}
acts as identity on the space of the data qubits. Therefore, we obtain that
X\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}}X^{-1}=\mathcal{D}_{\tilde{L}_{s}}
.

Thus, by performing the transformation given by
X
, the vectorized Lindbladian is now diagonal on the space of the data qubits. In particular, we can express

X\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\text{ref}}X^{-1}=\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{x,y},

where for
x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}
,
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}\in\text{L}(\mathbb{C}^{T+1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{T+1})
given by

\mathcal{A}_{x,y}=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s=0}^{T}f_{s}(x)\ket{s_{l}}\!\bra{s_{l}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s=0}^{T}f_{s}(y)\ket{s_{r}}\!\bra{s_{r}}+\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{\ket{s-1}\!\bra{s}+\text{h.c.}},

where
f_{s}(x)=x_{s+1}+x_{s+2}\dots x_{N}
if
0\leq s\leq N-1
and
f_{s}(x)=0
if
s\geq N
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}
is block diagonal — to see this, we perform the following decomposition

\mathbb{C}^{T+1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{T+1}=\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\cup\bigg(\bigcup_{t\in[1:T]}\mathcal{S}^{(1)}_{t}\bigg)\cup\bigg(\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in[0:T]\\
{|t-s|}\geq 2\end{subarray}}S^{(2)}_{t,s}\bigg),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{(0)}=\text{span}(\{\ket{t,t}\text{ for }t\in[0:T]\})\cong\mathbb{C}^{T+1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{(1)}_{t}=\text{span}\big(\{\ket{t,t-1},\ket{t-1,t}\}\big)\cong\mathbb{C}^{2}\text{ for }t\in[1:T],

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{(2)}_{t,s}=\text{span}\big(\{\ket{t,s}\}\big)\cong\mathbb{C}^{1}\text{ for }t,s\in[0:T]\text{ with }{|t-s|}\geq 2.

Then, we can note that
\forall x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}
,
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}
is block diagonal on the subspaces
\mathcal{S}^{(0)},\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(1)},\mathcal{S}_{t,s}^{(2)}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x,y}\big|_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}\cong-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s=0}^{N-1}\big(f_{s}(x)+f_{s}(y)\big)\ket{s}\!\bra{s}+\sum_{s=1}^{T}\bigg(\ket{s-1}\!\bra{s}+\ket{s-1}\!\bra{s}-\ket{s}\!\bra{s}-\ket{s-1}\!\bra{s-1}\bigg),

(90a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x,y}\big|_{\mathcal{S}^{(1)}_{t}}\cong-\bigg(2-\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{t,1}+\delta_{t,T})\bigg)I+\ket{1}\!\bra{0}+\ket{0}\!\bra{1}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\big(f_{t}(x)+f_{t-1}(x)\big)\ket{0}\!\bra{0}+\big(f_{t}(y)+f_{t-1}(y)\big)\ket{1}\!\bra{1}\bigg),

(90b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{x,y}\big|_{\mathcal{S}^{(2)}_{t,s}}\cong-\frac{1}{2}\big(4-\delta_{t,0}-\delta_{t,T}-\delta_{s,0}-\delta_{s,T}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\big(f_{t}(x)+f_{s}(y)\big).

(90c)

We now immediately obtain the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
, which are just the eigenvalues of the block diagonal operator
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}
. It can be noted that
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}|_{\mathcal{S}_{t}^{(1)}}\preceq-I/2
and
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}|_{\mathcal{S}_{t,s}^{(2)}}\leq-1
for any
x,y,s,t
. Finally, it also follows from Lemma
15
that only
\mathcal{A}_{x=0,y=0}|_{\mathcal{S}^{(0)}}
has a zero eigenvalue, with the eigenvector
|{\xi}\rrangle=(T+1)^{-1/2}(\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}+\ket{1_{l}1_{r}}+\dots\ket{T_{l}T_{r}})
. This allows us to obtain the fixed point of
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
(where an additional factor of
(T+1)^{-1/2}
is introduced to make the fixed point a normalized density matrix),

\displaystyle|{\sigma}\rrangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T+1}}VX\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T+1}}V_{l}V_{r}^{*}\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle,

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T+1}V\bigg((\ket{0}\!\bra{0})^{\otimes N}\otimes\sum_{t=0}^{T}\ket{t}\!\bra{t}\bigg)V^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\ket{\phi_{t}}\!\bra{\phi_{t}}\otimes\ket{t}\!\bra{t}.

Next, we bound
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}-\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}
. Out of the two transformations used to diagonalize
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
in the Hilbert space of the data qubits, the unitary
V
does not effect this norm i.e.

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}-\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}={|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}t}-\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\tilde{\sigma}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1},

(91)

where
\tilde{L}
is defined in Eq.
88
and
\tilde{\sigma}=(T+1)^{-1}(\ket{0}\!\bra{0})^{\otimes N}\otimes\sum_{t=0}^{T}\ket{t}\!\bra{t}
. Furthermore, we can note that, in the vectorized picture,
\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\tilde{\sigma}=|{\tilde{\sigma}}\rrangle\llangle{I}|
, where
|{\tilde{\sigma}}\rrangle=(T+1)^{-1}\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes\sum_{t=0}^{T}\ket{t_{l}t_{r}}=(T+1)^{-1/2}\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle
. Also note that
X|{\tilde{\sigma}}\rrangle=|{\tilde{\sigma}}\rrangle
and
\llangle{I}|X=\bra{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes\sum_{t=0}^{T}\bra{t_{l}t_{r}}=(T+1)^{1/2}\bra{0_{l}0_{r}}^{\otimes N}\otimes\llangle{\xi}|
.

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\text{ref}}t}-\text{Tr}(\cdot)\tilde{\sigma}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}

\displaystyle={\left\|X\bigg[\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes e^{\mathcal{A}_{x,y}t}\bigg]X-|{\tilde{\sigma}}\rrangle\llangle{I}|\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle={\left\|X\bigg[\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes e^{\mathcal{A}_{x,y}t}-\big(\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}\!\bra{0_{l}0_{r}}\big)^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle\!\llangle{\xi}|\bigg]X\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}^{2}{\left\|\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes e^{\mathcal{A}_{x,y}t}-\big(\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}\!\bra{0_{l}0_{r}}\big)^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle\!\llangle{\xi}|\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle\leq(2^{N}(T+1))^{1/2}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}^{2}{\left\|\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes e^{\mathcal{A}_{x,y}t}-\big(\ket{0_{l}0_{r}}\!\bra{0_{l}0_{r}}\big)^{\otimes N}\otimes|{\xi}\rrangle\!\llangle{\xi}|\right\|}_{2\to 2},

\displaystyle\leq(2^{N}(T+1))^{1/2}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}^{2}\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}{\left\|\ket{x_{l}y_{r}}\!\bra{x_{l}y_{r}}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{x,y}(t)\right\|}_{2\to 2},

\displaystyle=(2^{N}(T+1))^{1/2}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}^{2}\sum_{x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}}{\left\|\mathcal{Q}_{x,y}(t)\right\|}_{2\to 2}

(92)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{x,y}(t)=\begin{cases}e^{\mathcal{A}_{0^{N},0^{N}}t}-|{\xi}\rrangle\llangle{\xi}|&\text{ if }x=0^{N}\text{ and }y=0^{N},\\
e^{\mathcal{A}_{x,y}t}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that, for any superoperator
\mathcal{E}
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{E}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{2\to 2}
coincides with its operator norm when interpreted as an operator in the vectorized picture. This fact together with Lemma
15
allows us, for sufficiently large
T
, to provide the upper bound

{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{x,y}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{2\to 2}\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{4t}{T+1}\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2(T+1)}\bigg)\bigg)\ \forall x,y\in\{0,1\}^{N}.

Therefore, we obtain that

{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\text{ref}}t}-\textnormal{Tr}(\cdot)\tilde{\sigma}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq(T+1)^{1/2}2^{5N/2}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}^{2}\exp\bigg(-\frac{4t}{T+1}\sin^{2}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2(T+1)}\bigg)\bigg)

It remains to bound
{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}
(recall that
X
is interpreted as a superoperator) — we start with the simple upper bound

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|I\otimes\Pi_{N+1}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\bigg(\prod_{i=j}^{N}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X_{i}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\bigg){|\hskip-1.084pt|I\otimes\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq 1+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\bigg(1+\prod_{i=j}^{N}{|\hskip-1.084pt|X_{i}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\bigg){|\hskip-1.084pt|I\otimes\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}

(93)

The diamond norm
{|\hskip-1.084pt|X_{i}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}
can be computed explicitly. For this, we note that, as a single qubit superoperator,
X_{i}(\Omega)=\Omega+\bra{0_{i}}\Omega\ket{0_{i}}\otimes(\ket{0_{i}}\!\bra{0_{i}}-2\ket{1_{i}}\!\bra{1_{i}})
. Therefore, for
\Omega
on a possibly larger space,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|X_{i}(\Omega)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Omega|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\bra{0_{i}}\Omega\ket{0_{i}}\otimes(\ket{0_{i}}\!\bra{0_{i}}-2\ket{1_{i}}\!\bra{1_{i}})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Omega|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+3{|\hskip-1.084pt|\bra{0_{i}}\Omega\ket{0_{i}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Omega|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}
, which yields
{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 4
. Consequently, we obtain from Eq.
93
that

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq 1+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big(1+4^{N-j+1}\big){|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}.

(94)

Next, we provide upper bounds on
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\text{I}\otimes\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}
. Note first that
\Pi_{1}
, as a superoperator on
\mathbb{C}^{T+1}
, is a channel since it admits the Kraus representation

\Pi_{1}(X)=\sum_{s=0}^{T}\ket{s}\!\bra{s}X\ket{s}\!\bra{s}.

Therefore, we obtain that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{1}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 1
. Next, we note that
\Pi_{j}
for
j\in[2:N]
can be expressed as

\Pi_{j}(X)=\sum_{s=1}^{j-1}\bigg(\ket{j}\!\bra{j}X\ket{s}\!\bra{s}+\ket{s}\!\bra{s}X\ket{j}\!\bra{j}\bigg),

and consequently we obtain that

{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\Pi_{j}\otimes I)(X)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq\sum_{s=1}^{j-1}\big({|\hskip-1.084pt|\ket{j}\!\bra{j}X\ket{s}\!\bra{s}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\ket{s}\!\bra{s}X\ket{j}\!\bra{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\big)\leq 2(j-1)\implies{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2(j-1).

Using these bounds for
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}
together with Eq.
94
, we then obtain that

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|X|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq 1+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big(1+4^{N-j+1}\big)2(j-1)\leq 1+2N^{2}(1+4^{N})\leq 8N^{2}4^{N}.

(95)

Combining Eqs.
91
,
92
and
95
, we obtain the lemma statement.
∎

From Lemma
16
, it follows that starting from any initial state
\rho(0)
,
\rho(t)=e^{\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}t}\rho(0)
is an
\varepsilon
approximation to the fixed point
\sigma
given in Eq.
85
after time
t=\Theta(T^{3}\log(\varepsilon^{-1}))+\Theta(T^{3}\log T)+\Theta(T^{3}\log N)+\Theta(T^{3}N)
. Then, the fixed point can be used to effectively perform the quantum computation by first measuring the clock qudit on the computational basis and post-selecting on the measurement outcome being
\ket{T}
— if successful, the data qubits will then be in the state
\ket{\phi_{T}}
that would have been obtained at the output of the encoded quantum circuit. Since the probability of the clock qudit being in
\ket{T}
is
1/(T+1)
, this can be repeated
O(T)\leq O(\text{poly}(N))
to have successfully prepare
\ket{\phi_{T}}
on the data qubits.

C.2
2D local Lindbladian

Construction
. Consider a quantum circuit on
N
qubits, with depth
\textnormal{poly}(N)
. As described in Ref.
[
48
]
, we use the fact that any quantum circuit (Fig.
10
a) can be brought into a specific topology shown in Fig.
10
b, where a quantum gate first acts on qubits 1, 2, then on 2, 3, then on 3, 4 and so on, using SWAP gates and with at most a polynomial overhead in the depth. We assume that the quantum circuit, in expressed in this format, has depth
NR
, where
R
is
\text{poly}(N)
, where each round is composed of 1 single qubit and
N-1
two qubit gates. We will encode this circuit into a 2D dissipative system with only spatially local jump operators — we consider
NR
qudits with
d=6
arranged in a 2D grid with
N
rows and
R
columns.

Figure 9:
Schematic depiction of the circuit to geometrically local Lindbladian encoding. Given an arbitrary quantum circuit on
N
qubits and depth
\text{poly}(N)
(a), it can always be mapped to a quantum circuit on
N
qubits with architecture shown in (b) where the quantum gates are applied on the qubits sequentially and in several rounds. The total number of rounds needed for a circuit that was originally of depth
\text{poly}(N)
is at most
\text{poly}(N)
. Finally, this circuit is then encoded into a geometrically local Lindbladian on 2D grid of 6-level qudits as shown in (c).

The qudit internal states will be denoted by
\{\ket{0},\ket{1},\ket{\bar{0}},\ket{\bar{1}},\ket{\times},\ket{\odot}\}
. We make the following remarks about notation used in this subsection:

(a)

For
z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1},\times,\odot\}
,
\ket{z_{x,y}}
will mean that the qudit at the
x^{\text{th}}
column and
y^{\text{th}}
row is in the state
z
.

(b)

We will also define the operators
\pi=\ket{0}\!\bra{0}+\ket{1}\!\bra{1}
and
\bar{\pi}=\ket{\bar{0}}\!\bra{\bar{0}}+\ket{\bar{1}}\!\bra{\bar{1}}
, and
\pi_{x,y}
or
\bar{\pi}_{x,y}
denote the operators
\pi
or
\bar{\pi}
respectively when applied to the qudit in the
x^{\text{th}}
row and
y^{\text{th}}
column.

(c)

We will refer to the qudit being in
\ket{\times},\ket{\odot}
, barred
0/1
and unbarred
0/1
as being of distinct types. Thus, an operation that changes the ‘type’ of a qudit could be an operation that converts a qudit from
\times
to
\odot
or from barred
0/1
to
\times
etc. Likewise, an operation that simply rotates a qudit within the space of barred (or unbarred) 0/1 states will not change its type.

(d)

We will often use operators defined on qudits that are horizontally adjacent, vertically adjacent or arranged on a plaquette. It will be convenient and visually clearer to express tensor products of operators as a matrix of operators associated with the coordinate of the lower leftmost vertex. For example,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}D&C\\

{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 39.37 31.37 L 39.37 17.46 M 39.37 -8 L 39.37 -21.91}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 12.73 4.73 L 26.64 4.73 M 52.1 4.73 L 66.01 4.73}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

First, we map the quantum circuit on
N
qubits into a larger quantum circuit on the
NR
qudits with the property that we only ever apply a gate between two (horizontally or vertically) neighbouring qudits and every neighbouring pair of qudits experience a gate only once (note that any one qudit might experience multiple two-qudit gates, but they will always be with a different second qudit). Similar to the situation with Hamiltonians, this will avoid the need of an explicit clock qudit. Also similar to the situation with Hamiltonian encodings, we point out that removing the clock qudit to allow for a geometrically local Lindbladian would come at the cost of a much larger Hilbert space than that of the encoded qubits — consequently, only a small subspace of this large Hilbert space will be used for the encoded computation. We will provide a precise definition of this subspace later — in the following qualitative description of the how to construct the encoding Lindbladian, we will simply refer to as the “space of valid states”.

Figure 10:
Schematic depiction of the jump operators used to implement the circuit. (a) In the first round, the qudits are initially in the state shown in
\ket{\Psi_{0}}
. The gates are then applied through the jump operators
L_{1}^{(1)},L_{1,2}^{(1)},L_{2,3}^{(1)}\dots
which change the state of the qudits in the first column and also convert them from unbarred to barred states. (b) In the second round, the qudits are swapped from the first column to the second column by the jump operators
L_{1}^{(1,2)},L_{2}^{(1,2)}\dots
— simultaneously, the qudits in the first column are converted to
\times
and the qudits in the second column are converted to unbarred states. (c) Next, we apply the jump
L_{1}^{(2)},L_{1,2}^{(2)},L_{2,3}^{(2)}\dots
which execute the second round of gates. The gray-shaded qudits are the qudits whose state can be changed by the applied operation, and the pink-shaded qudits are qudits whose state does not change on the application of the jump operator but are checked to be in the valid state by each jump operator. In particular, a gray shaded qudit whose type is being changed is always surrounded by a gray or pink-shaded qudit in our construction.

Initially, all the qudits other than those in the first column will be in
\ket{\odot}
, all the qudits in the first column will be in
\ket{0}
:

\displaystyle\ket{\Psi}=\bigotimes_{x=1}^{N}\bigg(\ket{0_{x,1}}\otimes\bigotimes_{y=2}^{R}\ket{\odot_{x,y}}\bigg).

(96)

We then apply the first round of unitaries,
U_{1}^{(1)},U_{12}^{(1)},U_{23}^{(1)}\dots U_{N-1,N}^{(1)}
and simultaneously transform the qudit state from
\ket{a}\to\ket{\bar{a}}
for
a\in\{0,1\}
. This is accomplished by applying the operators:

\displaystyle L^{(1)}_{1}=\sum_{a,b=0}^{1}\big(U^{(1)}_{1}\big)_{a,b}\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\bar{a}}\!\bra{b}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{2,2}+\text{h.c.},

(97)

\displaystyle L^{(1)}_{y,y+1}=\begin{cases}\sum_{a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}=0}^{1}\big(U^{(1)}_{y,y+1}\big)_{a,a^{\prime};b,b^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\bar{a}}\!\bra{\bar{b}}&\\
\ket{\bar{a}^{\prime}}\!\bra{b}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{2,y+2}+\text{h.c.}&\text{ if }y\in[1:N-2],\\
\sum_{a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}=0}^{1}\big(U^{(1)}_{y,y+1}\big)_{a,a^{\prime};b,b^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\bar{a}}\!\bra{\bar{b}}&\\
\ket{\bar{a}^{\prime}}\!\bra{b}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{2,y+1}+\text{h.c.}&\text{ if }y=N-1\end{cases}

(98)

Note that these operators perform two tasks — (1) they apply the unitaries
U^{(1)}_{1},U^{(1)}_{1,2}\dots U^{(1)}_{N-1,N}
and (2) they also check if the qudit whose type is being changed is horizontally and vertically adjacent to a qudit in the correct “type”. After application of the operators
L_{1}^{(1)},L_{1,2}^{(1)}\dots L_{N-1,N}^{(1)}
, all the qudits in the first row will be in the states
\ket{\bar{0}},\ket{\bar{1}}
. We also note that the order in which the operators
L_{1}^{(1)},L_{1,2}^{(1)}\dots L_{n-1,n}^{(1)}
are applied does not matter if we apply sufficiently large number of them — this is because the operator
L_{x,x+1}^{(1)}
only applies
U^{(1)}_{x,x+1}
if the
i^{\text{th}}
qudit in the first column is in an barred state, and the
(x+1)^{\text{th}}
qudit is in an unbarred state, else it annihilates the state (i.e. maps the state to 0). Furthermore, only if this unitary is successfully applied would the
(x+1)^{\text{th}}
qudit will be mapped to a barred state, and consequently the only next operator that will act without annihilating the state is
V^{(1)}_{x+1,x+2}
. Consequently, the choice of operators in Eq.
97
naturally enforces the ordering of the unitaries in circuit being encoded. Another important point about the operators constructed in Eq.
97
is that the operators also check if the qudit whose type is being changed is horizontally and vertically adjacent to a valid type — in particular, this qudit should have a barred state vertically above it, an unbarred state vertically below it and a
\ket{\odot}
state horizontally to its right. While this check might seem vacuous if the initial state is valid, it becomes important if the initial state is invalid and needs to be brought into the valid subspace via additional jump operators (which we describe below).

Next, we apply operators that swap the barred states from the first column to unbarred states in the second column, and simultaneously map the states in the first column to
\ket{\times}
. This is accomplished by applying the operators

\displaystyle L_{y}^{(1,2)}=\begin{cases}\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\pi}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\bar{a}}&\ket{a}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{3,y+1}&\text{ if }y\in[2:N-1],\\
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\bar{a}}&\ket{a}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{3,y+2}&\text{ if }y=1,\\
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\pi}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\bar{a}}&\ket{a}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{3,y}&\text{ if }y=N,\end{cases}

(99)

In the next step, we again apply a round of operators similar to Eq.
97
on the second column, followed by operators similar to Eq.
99
on the second and third columns. More specifically, we apply the jump operators,

\displaystyle L^{(x)}_{1}=\sum_{a,b=0}^{1}\big(U^{(x)}_{1}\big)_{a,b}\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\bar{a}}\!\bra{b}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
&\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{x+1,2},L^{(x)}_{y,y+1}=\sum_{a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}=0}^{1}\big(U^{(x)}_{y,y+1})_{a,a^{\prime};b,b^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\bar{a}}\!\bra{\bar{b}}&\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\bar{a}^{\prime}}\!\bra{b^{\prime}}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
&\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{x+1,y+2},

(100a)

\displaystyle L^{(x,x+1)}_{y}=\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\begin{bmatrix}&\bar{\pi}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\bar{a}}&\ket{a}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\pi&\end{bmatrix}_{x+2,y+1}

(100b)

where any single qudit operator in these expressions which is defined outside the lattice should be treated as identity. For simplicity, we will often denote the set containing all of these jump operators by
\{L_{t}:t\in\mathcal{T}\}
, with the set
\mathcal{T}
indexing different jump operators.

We define
\mathcal{S}
as the set of states which are of the form shown in Fig.
11
with the barred or unbarred 0/1 qubits being in a possibly entangled state.
\mathcal{S}
contains the states that are obtained from the application of the jump operators
L^{(x)}_{y,y+1}
and
L^{(x,x+1)}_{y}
to the initial state
\ket{\Psi_{0}}
and will be referred to the space of “valid” configurations. Furthermore,
\mathcal{S}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes{N}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\otimes{2NR+1}}
i.e. every state in
\mathcal{S}
is a linear combination of states of the form
\ket{\psi}\otimes\ket{\gamma_{t}}
, where
\ket{\gamma_{t}}
for
t\in\{0,1,2\dots 2NR\}
indicates the “shape” of the state (i.e. which qudits are barred 0/1, unbarred 0/1,
\odot
or
\times
) and
\ket{\psi}
is a
N-
qubit entangled state. We can also think of the states
\{\ket{\gamma_{t}}\}_{t\in[0:(2nR+1)]}
as the state of a clock, even though we do not have any explicit qubits designated to be the clock. Similar to the 5-local case, we will need to penalize invalid configurations. As is shown Ref.
[
48
]
, the invalid configurations in the 2D encoding can be checked 2-locally. In particular, the show the following lemma.

Lemma 17

(Claim 4.2 of Ref.
[
48
]
)
.

A computational basis state
\ket{x}\in\{\times,\odot,0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}
is a valid state (i.e.
\ket{x}\in\mathcal{S}
) if none of the following two-qudit horizontally or vertically adjacent configurations are present in
\ket{x}
(
a
or
b
denotes unbarred
0
or
1
state and
\bar{a}
or
\bar{b}
denotes barred
0
or
1
state):

1.

(\odot,a)
,
(\odot,\bar{a})
, since in all valid states, a qudit in the state
\odot
is to the right of all the other qudits.

2.

(a,\times)
,
(\bar{a},\times)
, since in all valid states, a qudit in the state
\times
is to the left of all the other qudits.

3.

(\odot,\times)
,
(\times,\odot)
since in all valid states, qubits in
\times
and qubits in
\odot
are separated by a barred or unbarred qudit.

4.

(a,b)
,
(\bar{a},b)
,
(a,\bar{b})
or
(\bar{a},\bar{b})
