since there is only one qudit in a barred or unbarred 0/1 state per row.

5.

\big({\odot\atop\bar{a}}\big),\big({\times\atop\bar{a}}\big),\big({b\atop\bar{a}}\big)
since only a barred 0/1 qudit can be vertically above a barred 0/1 qudit.

6.

\big({a\atop\odot}\big)
,
\big({a\atop\times}\big)
since only an unbarred 0/1 qudit can be vertically below an unbarred 0/1 qudit.

7.

\big({\odot\atop\times}\big)
,
\big({\times\atop\odot}\big)
since qudits in
\odot
state and
\times
state cannot be vertically adjacent.

8.

\big({\bar{a}\atop\odot}\big)
,
\big({\times\atop a}\big)
since there is no
\odot
qudit below a barred 0/1 qudit and no
\times
qudit above an unbarred 0/1 qudit.

9.

\odot
in the first column of qudits and
\times
in the last column of qudits.

For each of the invalid configurations from 1 to 9, which we now construct a jump operator which maps the invalid qudits’ state to valid qudit states by just changing the state of one of the qudits. More importantly, for a set of two neighbouring vertical qudits that are in an invalid configuration, we will make the choice to change the bottom qudit to make the configuration valid and for a set of two neighbouring horizontal qudits that are in an invalid configuration, we will make the choice to change the left qudit to make the configuration valid. This can be accomplished by adding the jump operators shown in the table below corresponding to each of the two-qudit invalid configurations.

Configuration

Jump operator(s)

Configuration

Jump operator(s)

(\odot,a)

\ket{\odot,\odot}\!\bra{\odot,a}

\big({\odot\atop\bar{a}}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\left|{\odot\atop z}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{\odot\atop\bar{a}}\right|,\ z\in\{\odot,0,1\}

(\odot,\bar{a})

\ket{\odot,\odot}\!\bra{\odot,\bar{a}}

\big({\times\atop\bar{a}}\big)

\left|{\times\atop\times}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{\times\atop\bar{a}}\right|

(a,\times)

\ket{a,\odot}\!\bra{a,\times}

\big({b\atop\bar{a}}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left|{b\atop z}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{b\atop\bar{a}}\right|,\ z\in\{0,1\}

(\bar{a},\times)

\ket{\bar{a},\odot}\!\bra{\bar{a},\times}

\big({a\atop\odot}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left|{{a\atop z}}\right\rangle\!\bra{{a\atop\odot}},\ z\in\{0,1\}

(\odot,\times)

\ket{\odot,\odot}\!\bra{\odot,\times}

\big({a\atop\times}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left|{{a\atop z}}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{{a\atop\times}}\right|,\ z\in\{0,1\}

(\times,\odot)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\ket{\times,z}\!\bra{\times,\odot},\ z\in\{\times,0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}

\big({\odot\atop\times}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\left|{\odot\atop z}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{\odot\atop\times}\right|,\ z\in\{\odot,0,1\}

(a,b)

\ket{a,\odot}\!\bra{a,b}

\big({\times\atop\odot}\big)

\left|{{\times\atop\times}}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{\times\atop\odot}\right|

(\bar{a},b)

\ket{\bar{a},\odot}\!\bra{\bar{a},b}

\big({\bar{a}\atop\odot}\big)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\left|{\bar{a}\atop z}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{\bar{a}\atop\odot}\right|,z\in\{\times,0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}

(a,\bar{b})

\ket{a,\odot}\bra{a,\bar{b}}

\big({\times\atop a}\big)

\left|{{\times\atop\times}}\right\rangle\!\left\langle{{\times\atop{a}}}\right|

(\bar{a},\bar{b})

\ket{\bar{a},\odot}\!\bra{\bar{a},\bar{b}}

(\odot_{1,1})

\frac{1}{2}\ket{z_{1,1}}\bra{\odot_{1,1}}\ z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}

(\times_{R,1})

\frac{1}{2}\ket{z_{x,R}}\!\bra{\times_{1,R}}\text{ for }z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}

Table 3:
Jump operators corresponding to different invalid configurations. Each of the horizontal two-qudit jump operator corrects the invalid configuration by changing the right-most qudit, and the vertical two-qudit jump operator corrects the invalid configuration by changing the bottom-most qudit.

We will denote the set of jump operators penalizing these incorrect configurations by
\{{P}_{j}:j\in\mathcal{J}\}
— this set will include the horizontal and vertical two-qudit jump operators at every point on the lattice, as well as the single qudit jump operators on the first and last column of the lattice.

Figure 11:
Schematic depiction of typical valid states i.e. states that lie in the subspace
\mathcal{S}
and are reached on applying the jump operators
\{L_{t}:t\in\mathcal{T}\}
on the initial state
\ket{\Psi_{0}}
.

Finally, to initialize the qubits to
\ket{\Psi_{0}}
, we add jump operators
\{S_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[1:N]}
which act only on the first column of qudits and map them to unbarred 0 from unbarred 1 i.e
S_{\alpha}=\ket{0_{1,\alpha}}\!\bra{1_{1,\alpha}}
.

Analysis
. We begin by establishing that the operators
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
enforce that, at long times, the state of the qudits is in the valid subspace. For this, we consider the operator
F
, which counts the number of invalid configurations in the state of the qudits:

F=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j},

and analyze its dynamics in the Heisenberg picture. We will use the following technical lemma.

Lemma 18
.

Suppose
x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t),x_{3}(t)\dots x_{n}(t)\geq 0
, with
x_{i}(0)\leq 1\ \forall i\in[1:n]
, satisfy the inequalities

\frac{d}{dt}x_{1}(t)\leq-\alpha_{1}x_{1}(t)\text{ and }\frac{d}{dt}x_{i}(t)\leq-\alpha_{i}x_{i}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\beta_{i,j}x_{j}(t),

where
\alpha_{i}\geq\alpha>0
,
\beta_{i,j}\geq 0,\sum_{j}\beta_{i,j}\leq\beta
for some
\alpha,\beta>0
and
\forall i
, then

x_{i}(t)\leq(\max(1,2\beta/\alpha))^{n}e^{-\alpha t/2}\text{ for all }t\geq 0,i\in[1:n].

Proof.

We immediately obtain that
x_{1}(t)\leq x_{1}(0)e^{-\alpha_{1}t}\leq e^{-\alpha t}
. We can rewrite the inequalities for
x_{2}(t),x_{3}(t)\dots x_{n}(t)
as integral inequalities:

x_{i}(t)\leq e^{-\alpha_{i}t}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\beta_{i,j}\int_{0}^{t}x_{j}(s)e^{-\alpha_{i}(t-s)}ds\leq e^{-\alpha t}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\beta_{i,j}\int_{0}^{t}x_{j}(s)e^{-\alpha(t-s)}ds.

Define
\tilde{x}_{i}(t)=\max_{j\in[1:i]}x_{j}(t)
— we then obtain that
\tilde{x}_{1}(t)\leq e^{-\alpha t}
and for
i\geq 2
,

\tilde{x}_{i}(t)\leq e^{-\alpha t}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\beta_{i,j}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{x}_{i-1}(s)e^{-\alpha(t-s)}ds\leq e^{-\alpha t}+\beta\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{x}_{i-1}(s)e^{-\alpha(t-s)}ds.

We can integrate these equations recursively to obtain that
\forall i\in[1:n]
,

x_{i}(t)\leq\tilde{x}_{i}(t)\leq\bigg(1+\beta t+\frac{\beta^{2}t^{2}}{2!}+\frac{\beta^{3}t^{3}}{3!}+\dots\frac{\beta^{i-1}t^{i-1}}{(i-1)!}\bigg)e^{-\alpha t},

Noting that for
t\geq 0
and
i\in[1:n]
,
(\beta t)^{i}=(\alpha t/2)^{i}(2\beta/\alpha)^{i}\leq(\max(1,2\beta/\alpha))^{n}(\alpha t/2)^{i}
, we obtain that

x_{i}(t)\leq(\max(1,2\beta/\alpha))^{n}\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha t}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{2}t^{2}}{2!2^{2}}+\frac{\alpha^{3}t^{3}}{3!2^{3}}\dots\frac{\alpha^{i-1}t^{i-1}}{(i-1)!2^{i-1}}\bigg)e^{-\alpha t}\leq(\max(1,2\beta/\alpha))^{n}e^{-\alpha t/2},

which establishes the lemma statement.
∎

Lemma 19
.

For any initial state
\rho(0)
of the 2D grid of qudits,
\langle F(t)\rangle=\textnormal{Tr}(Fe^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho(0)))\leq(NR+1)20^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}
.

Proof.

It will be convenient to define the sets
\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(H)}=\{j\in\mathcal{J}:P_{j}\text{ is a 2-qudit operator supported on }(x-1,y),(x,y)\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(V)}=\{j\in\mathcal{J}:P_{j}\text{ is a 2-qudit operator supported on }(x,y-1),(x,y)\}
.
We first re-express
F
as

F=\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}+\pi^{\times}_{R,1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[1:R]\\
y\in[1:N]\end{subarray}}F_{x,y},

where
\pi^{\odot}_{x,y}=\ket{\odot_{x,y}}\!\bra{\odot_{x,y}}
,
\pi^{\times}_{x,y}=\ket{\times_{x,y}}\!\bra{\times_{x,y}}
and

\displaystyle F_{x,y}=\delta_{x\neq 1}F_{x,y}^{(H)}+\delta_{y\neq 1}F_{x,y}^{(V)}\text{ where }

\displaystyle\text{for }x\neq 1:F^{(H)}_{x,y}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(H)}}P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j}=\begin{bmatrix}\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}+\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\text{for }y\neq 1:F^{(V)}_{x,y}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(V)}}P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j}=\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+{\pi}\\
\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}+\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}+\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\pi\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}+\begin{bmatrix}\pi+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}.

(101)

Here, we define
\delta_{a\neq b}=1-\delta_{a,b}
. We also define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(H)}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(H)}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\text{ and }\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(V)}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(V)}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}.

(102)

As laid out in Table
3
, since for any horizontally (vertically) invalid two-qudit configuration, the horizontal (vertical) two-qudit jump operators
P_{j}
correct it by changing the right (bottom) qudit, it follows that

\displaystyle\text{If }(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\notin\{(x-1,y),(x,y)\},\text{ then }\forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}^{(H)}:[P_{j},F^{(H)}_{x,y}]=0\implies\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(F^{(H)}_{x,y})=0,

(103a)

\displaystyle\text{If }(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\notin\{(x,y-1),(x,y)\}\text{ then }\forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}^{(H)}:[P_{j},F^{(V)}_{x,y}]=0\implies\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(F^{(V)}_{x,y})=0,

(103b)

\displaystyle\text{If }(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\notin\{(x,y-1),(x,y)\}\text{ then }\forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}^{(V)}:[P_{j},F^{(V)}_{x,y}]=0\implies\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(F^{(V)}_{x,y})=0,

(103c)

\displaystyle\text{If }(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\notin\{(x-1,y),(x,y)\},\text{ then }\forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}^{(V)}:[P_{j},F^{(H)}_{x,y}]=0\implies\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}(F^{(H)}_{x,y})=0.

(103d)

Figure 12:
Schematic depiction of which jump operators
P_{j}
effect error number operators
F_{x,y}^{(H)},F_{x,y}^{(V)}
corresponding to a given edge. The edges whose associated
P_{j}
can change
F_{x,y}^{(H)},F_{x,y}^{(V)}
corresponding to a dark edge are shown in gray, while the
P_{j}
operators associated with the dotted edges do not impact the error number operator corresponding to the dark edge.

These relationships are depicted in Fig.
12
which shows, for a given horizontal or vertical edge associated with a
F_{x,y}^{(H/V)}
, which edges are associated with terms
\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(H/V)\dagger}
that result in a non-zero value when applied on the
F_{x,y}^{(H/V)}
. Furthermore, it can be verified by explicit computation that

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\mathcal{D}_{\ket{z_{R,1}}\!\bra{\times_{R,1}}}^{\dagger}\left(F^{(H)}_{R,1}\right)=0.

(104)

It can also be noted that, as a consequence of the specific choice of
L_{t}
(Eqs.
100
) which checks the validity of the qudits horizontally and vertically adjacent to the qudit whose type is being changed,

\displaystyle[F_{x,y}^{(H)},L_{t}]=0=[F_{x,y}^{(V)},L_{t}]\ \forall\ t\in\mathcal{T}.

(105)

Furthermore, since
F^{(H)}_{x,y}
and
F^{(V)}_{x,y}
are expressible entirely in terms of the projector
\pi=\ket{0}\!\bra{0}+\ket{1}\!\bra{1}
, as opposed to the individual projectors
\ket{0}\!\bra{0}
and
\ket{1}\!\bra{1}
, it also follow that

\displaystyle[F_{x,y}^{(H)},S_{\alpha}]=0=[F_{x,y}^{(V)},S_{\alpha}]\ \forall\alpha\in\mathcal{A}.

(106)

We now obtain upper bounds on
\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle=\textnormal{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(H)}\rho(t))
and
\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle=\textnormal{Tr}(F^{(V)}_{x,y}\rho(t))
. It follows from Eqs.
103
,
104
,
105
and
106
that
\forall x\in[2:R],y\in[1:N]
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta_{x\neq 2}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x-1,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))+\delta_{y\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))+\delta_{y\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x-1,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\frac{1}{4}\delta_{x,2}\delta_{y,1}\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big(\mathcal{D}_{\ket{z_{1,1}}\!\bra{\odot_{1,1}}}^{\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t)\big),

(107a)

and
\forall x\in[1:R],y\in[2:N]
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle=\delta_{y\neq 2}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x,y-1}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))+\delta_{x\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))+\delta_{x\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x,y-1}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\frac{1}{4}\delta_{x,1}\delta_{y,2}\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big({\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\ket{z_{1,1}}\!\bra{\odot_{1,1}}}}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t)\big)+\frac{1}{4}\delta_{x,R}\delta_{y,2}\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big({\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\ket{z_{R,1}}\!\bra{\times_{R,1}}}}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t)\big)

(107b)

where we have used the fact that
\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(H)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})=-F_{x,y}^{(H)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(V)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})=-F_{x,y}^{(V)}
. We can upper bound some of the terms on the right hand side of Eqs.
107
a and b with a simple argument — note that

\displaystyle\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x-1,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{(H)}_{x-1,y}}\text{Tr}(P_{j}^{\dagger}F_{x,y}^{(H)}P_{j}\rho(t))-\textnormal{Tr}(F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}F_{x,y}^{(H)}\rho(t)),

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{(H)}_{x-1,y}}\text{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(H)}P_{j}\rho(t)P_{j}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{(H)}_{x-1,y}}\text{Tr}(P_{j}\rho(t)P_{j}^{\dagger})=\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle,

(108)

where in (1) we have used the fact that
F^{(H)}_{x-1,y}F^{(H)}_{x,y}\succeq 0
, and in (2) we have used the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|F|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{x,y}^{(H)}\leq 1
and consequently for any
\sigma\succeq 0,\text{Tr}(F^{(H)}_{x,y}\sigma)\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|F_{x,y}^{(H)}|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq\text{Tr}(\sigma)
. Proceeding similarly, we can obtain the upper bounds

\displaystyle\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x-1,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t)),\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x,y-1}(F^{(V)}_{x,y})\rho(t))\leq\langle F_{x,y-1}^{(V)}(t)\rangle,\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x-1,y}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))\leq\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle.

(109)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big({\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\ket{z_{1,1}}\!\bra{\odot_{1,1}}}}(F_{1,2}^{(V)})\rho(t)\big),\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big({\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\ket{z_{1,1}}\!\bra{\odot_{1,1}}}}(F_{2,1}^{(H)})\rho(t)\big)\leq 4\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle,

(110)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\{0,1,\bar{0},\bar{1}\}}\text{Tr}\big({\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\ket{z_{R,1}}\!\bra{\times_{R,1}}}}(F_{R,2}^{(V)})\rho(t)\big)\leq 4\langle\pi^{\times}_{R,1}(t)\rangle

(111)

Using these bounds and Eq.
107
, we obtain that
\forall\ x\in[2:R],y\in[1:N]
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{x\neq 2}\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\delta_{y\neq 1}\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle+\delta_{y\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))+\delta_{x,2}\delta_{y,1}\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle,

(112a)

and
\forall x\in[1:R],y\in[2:N]
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle\leq\delta_{y\neq 2}\langle F_{x,y-1}^{(V)}(t)\rangle+\delta_{x\neq 1}\langle F_{x,y-1}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\delta_{x\neq 1}\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))+\delta_{x,1}\delta_{y,2}\langle\pi_{1,1}^{\odot}(t)\rangle+

\displaystyle\delta_{x,R}\delta_{y,2}\langle\pi^{\times}_{R,1}(t)\rangle.

(112b)

We will now arrange these inequalities into a sequence which is of the form analyzed in Lemma
18
, and then use this lemma to obtain an upper bound on
\langle F(t)\rangle
. We will first consider the first column of qudits and upper bound
\langle F^{(V)}_{1,y}(t)\rangle
, as well as
\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle
. For
\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle+\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle=0.

(113)

Specializing Eq.
112
b to the first column of qudits (
x=1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{1,y}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{1,y}(t)\rangle\leq\delta_{y\neq 2}\langle F_{1,y-1}(t)\rangle+\delta_{y,2}\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle,

(114)

where we have used the fact that
F_{1,y}=F_{1,y}^{(V)}
.

We next consider inequalities for all the remaining columns. For
\langle F_{x,1}^{(H)}(t)\rangle=\langle F_{x,1}(t)\rangle
, we obtain from Eq.
112
a that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,1}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,1}(t)\rangle\leq\delta_{x\neq 2}\langle F_{x-1,1}(t)\rangle+\delta_{x,2}\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle.

(115)

To use Eqs.
112
a, b when
y\in[2:N]
, we need to provide upper bounds on
\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(H)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))
and
\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}^{(V)\dagger}_{x,y}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))
. While these terms could be bounded by following the argument in Eq.
108
, the bound thus obtained is too loose to be useful. Instead, a more careful analysis is needed — in particular, one important fact that we will use is that if
F_{x-1,y}^{(V)}
and
F_{x,y-1}^{(H)}
have small expected value (i.e. the invalid configurations on the edges
((x-1,y-1),(x-1,y))
and
((x-1,y-1),(x,y-1))
) are unlikely), then the probable configurations on the diagonally opposite sites
(x-1,y),(x,y-1)
are also constrained. This can be made formal by noting the simple inequality

\displaystyle\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ \end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ \end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ \end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y-1}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}_{x-1,y}\rho(t)\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\langle F^{(H)}_{x,y-1}(t)\rangle+\langle F^{(V)}_{x-1,y}(t)\rangle.

(116)

Equation
116
can be interpreted as indicating that if the configurations on the edges
((x-1,y-1),(x-1,y))
and
((x-1,y-1),(x,y-1))
are valid (i.e.
\langle F^{(H)}(x,y)\rangle=\langle F^{(V)}_{x,y}\rangle=0
), then the diagonally opposite vertices,
(x-1,y)
and
(x,y-1)
, cannot have the configurations
(a,b)
where
a\in\{\odot,\bar{0},\bar{1},0,1\}
and
b\in\{\times,\bar{0},\bar{1},0,1\}
. We now consider upper bounding
\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(V)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))
:

\displaystyle\textnormal{Tr}(\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(V)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(H)})\rho(t))=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(V)}}\text{Tr}(P_{j}^{\dagger}F_{x,y}^{(H)}P_{j}\rho(t))-\text{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(H)}F_{x,y}^{(V)}\rho(t)),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\bar{\pi}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\frac{2}{3}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\pi+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\bar{\pi}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)-\textnormal{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(H)}F_{x,y}^{(V)}\rho(t)),

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{3}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\bar{\pi}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\bar{\pi}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg),

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\bar{\pi}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg)+\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ &\bar{\pi}+\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\pi+\bar{\pi}+\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}&\ \end{bmatrix}_{x,y}\rho(t)\bigg),

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{3}\langle F_{x,y}^{(V)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y-1}^{(V)}(t)\rangle,

(117a)

where to obtain (1), we need to use the explicit expression for
F_{x,y}^{(H)}F_{x,y}^{(V)}
to cancel terms from
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{(V)}_{x,y}}P_{j}^{\dagger}F_{x,y}^{(H)}P_{j}
, and in (2) we have used the bound from Eq.
116
. Proceeding similarly, we can upper bound
\text{Tr}(\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(H)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))
:

\displaystyle\text{Tr}(\mathcal{L}_{x,y}^{(H)\dagger}(F_{x,y}^{(V)})\rho(t))=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{x,y}^{(H)}}\text{Tr}(P_{j}^{\dagger}F_{x,y}^{(V)}P_{j}\rho(t))-\textnormal{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(V)}F_{x,y}^{(H)}\rho(t)),

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y}+\frac{3}{5}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\frac{4}{3}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y}-\textnormal{Tr}(F_{x,y}^{(V)}F_{x,y}^{(H)}\rho(t)),

\displaystyle\leq\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y}+\frac{3}{5}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\\
\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y},

\displaystyle\leq\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}&\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}+\pi+\bar{\pi}\\
\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}+\pi+\bar{\pi}&\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y}+\frac{3}{5}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\ket{\times}\!\bra{\times}&\ket{\odot}\!\bra{\odot}\end{bmatrix}\rho(t)\bigg)_{x,y},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{5}\langle F_{x,y}^{(H)}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x-1,y}^{(H)}\rangle+\langle F_{x,y-1}^{(V)}(t)\rangle.

(117b)

From Eqs.
117
a, b together with Eqs.
112
a, b, we obtain that for
x\in[2:R-1],y\in[2:N]

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle F_{x,y}(t)\rangle+\frac{2}{5}\langle F_{x,y}(t)\rangle\leq 2\big(\langle F_{x-1,y}(t)\rangle+\langle F_{x,y-1}(t)\rangle\big)+\delta_{x,R}\delta_{y,2}\langle\pi^{\times}_{R,1}(t)\rangle.

(118)

Finally, we can similarly obtain an inequality for
\langle\pi_{1,R}^{\times}(t)\rangle
from

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle\pi_{R,1}^{\times}(t)\rangle+\langle\pi_{1,R}^{\times}(t)\rangle\leq\text{Tr}\big(\mathcal{L}_{R,1}^{(H)\dagger}(\pi_{R,1}^{\times})\rho(t)\big)\leq\langle F_{R,1}(t)\rangle.

(119)

Now, we can see that the inequalities in Eqs.
113
,
114
,
115
,
118
and
119
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
18
with
x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t),x_{3}(t)\dots x_{n}(t)
being chosen as
\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{1,2}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{1,3}(t)\rangle\dots\langle F_{1,N}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{2,1}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{2,2}(t)\rangle\dots\langle F_{2,N}(t)\rangle\dots\langle F_{R-1,1}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{R-1,2}(t)\rangle\dots\langle F_{R-1,N}(t)\rangle
,
\langle F_{R,1}(t)\rangle,\langle\pi^{\times}_{R,1}(t)\rangle,\langle F_{R,2}(t)\rangle\dots\langle F_{R,N}(t)\rangle
and with
\alpha=2/5,\beta=4
. We thus obtain from
18
that

\langle F_{x,y}(t)\rangle,\langle\pi^{\odot}_{1,1}(t)\rangle,\langle\pi^{\times}_{R,1}(t)\rangle,\leq 20^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}\implies\langle F(t)\rangle\leq(NR+1)20^{NR+1}e^{-t/5},

which proves the lemma.
∎

We can now finally prove Lemma
7
from the main text.

Lemma
7
, repeated
.

Suppose we are given a quantum circuit
\mathcal{C}
on
N
qubits with architecture shown in Fig. depth and with
R
rounds of gates. Then, there exists a two-dimensional spatially local Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}
on
NR
6-level qudits with a unqiue fixed point
\sigma
, as well as a local observable
O
such that

\textnormal{Tr}(O\sigma)=\frac{1}{2NR}z_{\mathcal{C}},

where
z_{\mathcal{C}}
is the expected value of a pauli-
Z
operator on the last qubit at the output of
\mathcal{C}
. Furthermore, for any initial state
\rho(0)
of the two-dimensional grid of qudits,

{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho(0))-\sigma_{\mathcal{C}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq c_{0}(N,R)\exp(-\gamma_{0}(N,R)t),

where
c_{0}(N,R)=O(N^{6}R^{2}\exp(O(NR)))
and
\gamma_{0}(N,R)=\Theta(N^{-3}R^{-3})
.

Proof.

We first bound the 1-norm distance between
\rho(t)=e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho_{0})
and
\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(t)\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
to show that as
t\to\infty
, due to the action of the penalizing jump operators,
\rho(t)
evolves to be almost supported in the valid subspace
\mathcal{S}
. For this, we will use Lemma
19
— note that by definition of
\mathcal{S}
, a state
\ket{\psi}\in\mathcal{S}^{\perp}
is orthogonal to the kernel of at least one of the projectors
P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j}
and thus
\bra{\psi}F\ket{\psi}\geq\bra{\psi}P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j}\ket{\psi}=\bra{\psi}\psi\rangle
. Also, we observe that
F-\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\succeq 0
since for any
\ket{\psi}\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\bra{\psi}\big(F-\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\big)\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle=\bra{\psi}F\ket{\psi}-\bra{\psi}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\ket{\psi},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}\bra{\psi}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}F\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\ket{\psi}-\bra{\psi}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\ket{\psi},

\displaystyle\geq\bra{\psi}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\ket{\psi}-\bra{\psi}\Pi_{S^{\perp}}\ket{\psi}=0,

where, in (1) we have used the fact that, since
P_{j}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}=0\ \forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}
,
F\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}F=0\implies\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}F\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}=F
. Using
F\succeq\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}
, we obtain that

\textnormal{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}\rho(t))\leq\textnormal{Tr}(F\rho(t))\leq(NR+1)20^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}.

Suppose for
\rho\in\text{D}_{1}((\mathbb{C}^{6})^{\otimes NR})
,
\rho(t)=e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho)
, then

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|\rho(t)-\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(t)\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}\rho(t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(t)\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\textnormal{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}\rho(t))\textnormal{Tr}(\rho(t))}+\sqrt{\textnormal{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}\rho(t))\textnormal{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(t))},

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\textnormal{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}^{\perp}}\rho(t))}\leq 2(NR+1)^{1/2}20^{(NR+1)/2}e^{-t/10},

(120)

Next, it is convenient to introduce the superoperators
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}
defined by

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(X)=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\text{ and }\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}=\text{id}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}.

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
can be interpreted to project an operator onto another operator that is entirely supported on
\mathcal{S}
. Now, the master equation can be expressed as the following two equations:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho)=\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho)+\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho),

(121a)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho)=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho)+\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho),

(121b)

Furthermore, since
\forall\ j\in\mathcal{J}:P_{j}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}=0
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(X)=P_{j}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}P_{j}^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{2}\{P_{j}^{\dagger}P_{j},\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\}=0.

(122)

Since
\forall\ \alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathcal{T}:[L_{t},\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}]=[S_{\alpha},\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}]=0
,
\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}
,
\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}
. Thus, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}},

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}=0,

where we have used that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=0
. Similarly,

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}

\displaystyle={\left\|\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle={\left\|\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle={\left\|\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\big)\right\|}_{1\to 1},

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bigg({|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}+{\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|}_{1\to 1}\bigg),

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}2\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}4\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|P_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}^{2}\leq 4{|\mathcal{J}|},

where in (1) we use that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq 1
and in (2) we use that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{P_{j}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq 2{|\hskip-1.084pt|P_{j}|\hskip-1.084pt|}^{2}=2
. Finally, it also follows that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{D}_{\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}L_{t}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{D}_{\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}S_{\alpha}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}

\forall\ t\in\mathcal{T},\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
. To see this, note that since any
A\in\{S_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\cup\{L_{t}\}_{t\in\mathcal{T}}
commutes with
\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{S}\mathcal{D}_{A}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(X)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}-\frac{1}{2}\big(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}+\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\big),

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}-\frac{1}{2}\big(\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}+\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}X\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\big),

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}_{\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}A\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}(X).

(123)

From Eqs.
122
and
123
, it then follows that

\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}:=\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{S_{\alpha}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{D}_{L_{t}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}S_{\alpha}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{D}_{\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}L_{t}\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}}.

Furthermore,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}
, which is a Lindbladian superoperator corresponding to the restriction of the full Linbladian
\mathcal{L}
onto the valid subspace of states, is by construction unitarily equivalent to
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
with each gate (including the identity gates) in Fig.
10
b treated as an independent time-step.

Using Eq.
121
, we can now bound
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho_{0})-\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}
. From Eq.
121
a, we obtain that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))=e^{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}}(\rho_{0})
and from Eq.
121
b, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))=e^{\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}t}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(t-t^{\prime})}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime}.

(124)

Now, since
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}
is unitarily equivalent to
\mathcal{L}_{\text{ref}}
, we can use Lemma
16
to obtain that for any
A\in\text{L}((\mathbb{C}^{6})^{\otimes NR};\mathcal{S})

e^{\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{S}}t}(A)=\text{Tr}(A)\sigma+\chi_{t}(A),\text{ where }{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}\leq c_{0}(T,N)e^{-a_{0}(T)t},

where
T=2NR
and
c_{0}(T,N),a_{0}(T)
are defined in Lemma
16
. With this decomposition, we obtain from Eq.
124
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))

\displaystyle=\bigg[\text{Tr}(\mathcal{P}_{S}(\rho_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))\big)dt^{\prime}\bigg]\sigma+\chi_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}\bigg[\text{Tr}(\mathcal{P}_{S}(\rho_{0}))-\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}\big(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))\big)dt^{\prime}\bigg]\sigma+\chi_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{=}}\bigg[\text{Tr}(\mathcal{P}_{S}(\rho_{0}))-\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))\bigg)dt^{\prime}\bigg]\sigma+\chi_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}(\rho_{0})\sigma-\text{Tr}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t)))+\chi_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime},

where in (1) we have used the fact that
\text{Tr}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}(\cdot))=\text{Tr}(\mathcal{L}(\cdot))-\text{Tr}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}(\cdot))=-\text{Tr}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}(\cdot))
, since every operator in the image of a Lindbladian is traceless and in (2), we have used Eq.
121
b. Noting that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(0))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}={|\hskip-1.084pt|\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(0)\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1
, from Eq
120
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 2(NR+1)^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}
, and we obtain

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))-\text{Tr}(\rho_{0})\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(0)))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))dt^{\prime}\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq 2({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(0))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+\int_{0}^{t}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t^{\prime}))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle\leq 2({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(0))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+\int_{0}^{t}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\chi_{t-t^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1\to 1}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\rho(t^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle\leq 2({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}+c_{0}(T,N)e^{-a_{0}(T)t}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(0))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+8{|\mathcal{J}|}({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}c_{0}(T,N)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-a_{0}(T)(t-t^{\prime})}e^{-t^{\prime}/5}dt^{\prime},

\displaystyle\leq 2({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}+c_{0}(T,N)e^{-a_{0}(T)t}+\frac{40{|\mathcal{J}|}(NR+1)^{1/2}10^{NR+1}c_{0}(T,N)}{1-5a_{0}(T)}\big(e^{-t/5}-e^{-a_{0}(T)t}\big),

Finally, we obtain from
{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho_{0})-\text{Tr}(\rho_{0})\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}}(\rho(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}
that

{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho_{0})-\text{Tr}(\rho_{0})\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 4({NR+1})^{1/2}10^{NR+1}e^{-t/5}+c_{0}(T,N)e^{-a_{0}(T)t}+\frac{40{|\mathcal{J}|}(NR+1)^{1/2}10^{NR+1}c_{0}(T,N)}{1-5a_{0}(T)}\big(e^{-t/5}-e^{-a_{0}(T)t}\big).

From this bound, we obtain that as
N,R\to\infty
,

{|\hskip-1.084pt|e^{\mathcal{L}t}(\rho_{0})-\text{Tr}(\rho_{0})\sigma|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq O(N^{6}R^{2}\exp(O(NR)))\exp\big(-\Omega(N^{-3}R^{-3})t\big).

Finally, we construct a local observable whose expected value is the expected value of the pauli
Z
-operator on the last qubit at the output of the quantum circuit — this local observable will be
O=\ket{\bar{1}_{R,N}}\!\bra{\bar{1}_{R,N}}-\ket{\bar{0}_{R,N}}\!\bra{\bar{0}_{R,N}}
and it acts on the lower right-most qudit on the 2D grid. To see that this observable indeed measures the pauli
Z
-operator on the last qubit of the encoded circuit, recall from Lemma
16
that the fixed point of the Lindbladian contains a mixture of the (valid) state of the qudits corresponding to different time-steps of the encoded circuit. Note that in all of these time-steps except for the last one, the lower rightmost qudit is in the state
\ket{\times}
or in an unbarred
0/
state and thus yields a
0
expected value for the observable
O
. Consequently, the expected value of
O
in the fixed point only has a contribution from the state at the last time-step and is equal to the expected value of the pauli-
Z
on the last qubit of the encoded circuit multiplied by a normalization factor of
1/2NR
, which arises from the fact that the fixed point contains a convex combination of the state of the qudits at all time-steps.
∎

Appendix D
Stability to noise and errors

As detailed in the main text, we will asssume that the quantum simulator due to noise, instead of implementing
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
, will implement the Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\delta}

\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\delta}=\mathcal{L}_{\omega}+\delta\sum_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{\beta},

where
\mathcal{N}_{\beta}
, which itself is a Lindbladian, acts on the system qudits and ancilla qubits and is supported on
{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}
and
\delta
is the noise rate. We will assume that there is a
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\geq 0
such that

{|\{\beta^{\prime}:S^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S^{\prime}_{\beta}\neq\emptyset\}|}+{|\{\alpha:S^{\prime}_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset\}|}\leq\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\ \text{for all }\beta.

i.e.
{S}_{\beta}^{\prime}
intersects with at most
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
other subsets
S^{\prime}_{\beta}
or
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
(on which the jump operators
L_{\alpha}
and Hamiltonian terms
h_{\alpha}
corresponding to the target Lindbladian are supported). In particular, this implies that any one ancilla qubit is in the support of at most
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
Lindbladians
\mathcal{N}_{\beta}
. We will denote by
\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,s)=\exp(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\delta}(t-s))
and
\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)=\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)\rho(0)
, where we will again assume the ancillae to initially be in the
\ket{0}
state. We first modify the remainder expression given in Lemma
1
for the noiseless case.

Lemma
8
, repeated
.

If the ancillae are initially in
\ket{0}
then

\frac{d}{dt}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))-\omega^{2}\mathcal{L}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))=\mathcal{R}_{\omega}(t)+\delta\sum_{\beta}\mathcal{K}^{(0)}_{\beta}(t)+\delta\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\left(\omega\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta}(s)+\omega^{2}\mathcal{K}^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta}(s)\right)ds,

where
\mathcal{R}_{\omega}(t)
is as defined in Lemma
1
but with
\rho_{\omega}\to\rho_{\omega,\delta}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(t)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(1)}(t)

\displaystyle=-i[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)))]+\textnormal{h.c.},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(2)}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\textnormal{h.c.}.

Proof.

We follow the method as in the proof of Lemma
1
. We begin by writing the equations of motion for
\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))
and
\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))=-\omega\sum_{\alpha}(i[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}{\rho}_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\textnormal{h.c.})-i\omega^{2}[H_{\textnormal{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}({\rho}_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\delta\sum_{\beta}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))),

(125a)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))

\displaystyle\quad=-2\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))-i\omega L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))-i\omega^{2}[H_{\text{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\omega\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}{E_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t)}+\delta\sum_{\beta}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))).

(125b)

We now consider the remainder
\mathcal{R}_{\omega,\delta}(t)
— using the definition of the remainder (Eq.
10
) together with Eq.
8
a, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\omega,\delta}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))-\omega^{2}\mathcal{L}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha}\left(\left(-i\omega[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}{\rho}_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\text{h.c}\right)-\omega^{2}\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}\textnormal{Tr}_{A}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))\right)+\delta\sum_{\beta}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))).

(126)

Integrating Eq.
125b
, we find

\displaystyle\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\bigg(-i\omega^{2}[H_{\text{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega}(s))]-i\omega L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega}(s))+\omega\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}E_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)+\delta\sum_{\beta}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)))\bigg)ds.

(127)

Using integration by parts and the expression for
\frac{d}{dt}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))
from Eq.
125a
, we obtain

\displaystyle-i\omega\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))ds=-i\frac{\omega}{2}L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))+i\frac{\omega}{2}e^{-2t}\rho(0)+i\frac{\omega}{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}L_{\alpha}\frac{d}{ds}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))ds,

\displaystyle\quad=-i\frac{\omega}{2}L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))+i\frac{\omega}{2}e^{-2t}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho(0))-\frac{\omega^{3}}{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}L_{\alpha}[H_{\textnormal{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))]ds+\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i\frac{\omega\delta}{2}\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)))ds.

(128)

Inserting Eq.
128
into Eq.
127
, we obtain

\displaystyle-i\omega[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]+\frac{\omega^{2}}{2}e^{-2t}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}\rho(0)]

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2(t-s)}\bigg(-\omega^{3}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},[H_{\textnormal{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]-i\frac{\omega^{4}}{2}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}[H_{\textnormal{sys}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))]]\bigg)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\left(-i\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\bigg(\omega^{2}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},E_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)]+\frac{\omega^{3}}{2}[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)]\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\left(-i\omega\delta[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)))]+\frac{\omega^{2}\delta}{2}L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)))\right)ds,

(129)

Using Eq.
129
and Eq.
126
, we note that
-[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))]/2+\text{h.c}=\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}
and so arrive at

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\omega,\delta}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha}\left(\omega^{2}e^{-2t}q_{\alpha}+\omega^{4}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\left({\mathcal{Q}^{(1)}_{\alpha,H_{\textnormal{sys}}}(s)}+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\alpha,H_{\textnormal{sys}}}(s)\right)ds\right)+\omega^{4}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\big({\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)}+\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s)\big)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\omega^{2}\delta\sum_{\beta}\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(t)+\delta\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\left(\omega\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(1)}(s)+\omega^{2}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(2)}(s)\right),

(130)

where
q_{\alpha}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,H_{sys}}^{(1)}(t)
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,H_{sys}}^{(2)}(t)
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(3)}(t)
, and
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(4)}(t)
are defined in Lemma
1
(with the substitution
\rho_{\omega}(t)\to\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)
) and
\mathcal{K}^{(0)}_{\beta}(t)
,
\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)
,
\mathcal{K}^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta}(t)
are defined in the statement of this lemma.
∎

Next we provide bounds on the excitation of the ancillae in the presence of noise. The following lemma is the counterpart of Lemma
2
in the noiseless case.

Lemma
9
, repeated
.

Suppose
\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)
is the joint state of the system and the ancilla qubits with the ancilla qubits initially being in the state
\ket{0}
, then for all
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq\frac{\omega}{2}+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\text{ and }{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{\dagger}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq\frac{\omega^{2}}{4}+\frac{\omega\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{2}+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta.

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as the proof of Lemma
2
. We will denote by
|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle
the vectorization of
\ket{0}\!\bra{0}
on all the ancilla qubits. It will be convenient to note the following for
\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t)=\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}^{-1}(t,0)\sigma_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)
. We can now obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}^{-1}(t,0)[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\delta}]\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0),

\displaystyle=-2\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t)-i\omega\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}^{-1}(t,0)[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\sigma_{\alpha,l}^{\dagger}]L_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)+\delta\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}^{-1}(t,0)[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\mathcal{N}]\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0).

This can be integrated to obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)=\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)\sigma_{\alpha,l}e^{-2t}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,s)\big(-i\omega[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\sigma_{\alpha,l}^{\dagger}]L_{\alpha,l}+\delta[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\mathcal{N}]\big)\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(s,0)ds.

(131)

Consider now upper bounding
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}
. We can first upper bound

{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\llangle{\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\sigma_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},

where
\llangle{\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\sigma_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle
is interpreted as a superoperator on the system qudits. Using Eq.
131
and the fact that
\sigma_{\alpha,l}|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle=0
, we obtain that,

\displaystyle{\left\|\llangle{\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\sigma_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle\right\|}_{\diamond}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\bigg(\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|\llangle{\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,s)[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\sigma_{\alpha,l}^{\dagger}]L_{\alpha,l}\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(s,0)|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}+\delta{\left\|\llangle{\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,s)[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\mathcal{N}]\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(s,0)]|{0_{\mathcal{A}}}\rrangle\right\|}_{\diamond}\bigg)ds,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\bigg(\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\sigma_{\alpha,l}^{\dagger}]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt|L_{\alpha,l}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}+\delta{|\hskip-1.084pt|[\sigma_{\alpha,l},\mathcal{N}]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\bigg)ds,

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)}\big(\omega+2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\big)ds=\frac{\omega}{2}+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta,

where in (1) we have used the fact that at most only
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
terms
\mathcal{N}_{\beta}
do not commute with
\sigma_{\alpha,l}
i.e.
{|\{\beta:[\mathcal{N}_{\beta},\sigma_{\alpha,l}]\neq 0\}|}\leq\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
.

We follow a similar method to derive a bound on
\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{\dagger}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))
and
\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{\dagger}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))
. For
u\in\{-,+\}
, we write in vectorized notation,

\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))=\llangle{\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}|{\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t)}\rrangle=\llangle{\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(t)|{\rho(0)}\rrangle,

(132)

where we interpret the subcripts as
-=l,+=r
. We have that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t))=[\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t),\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\delta}(t)],

\displaystyle\quad=-4\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t)-i\omega L_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(t)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}^{z}\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)+ui\omega L_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{z}(t)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(t)+\delta[\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(t),\mathcal{N}(t)],

(133)

which can be integrated to obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(t)\sigma_{\alpha,l}(t)=e^{-4t}\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-4(t-s)}\left(-i\omega L_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}^{z}(s)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)+ui\omega L_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{z}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s)+\delta[\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s),\mathcal{N}(s)]\right)ds.

(134)

Inserting Eq.
134
into Eq.
132
and applying the fact that
\sigma_{\alpha,l}|{\rho(0)}\rrangle=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(t))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-4(t-s)}\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|\llangle{\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)L_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}^{z}(s)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)|{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-4(t-s)}\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|\llangle{\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)L_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{z}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s)|{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-4(t-s)}\delta{|\hskip-1.084pt|\llangle{\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}}|\mathcal{E}_{\omega,\delta}(t,0)[\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}(s)\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l}(s),\mathcal{N}(s)]|{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}ds.

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-4(t-s)}\left(\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}|{\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+\omega{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha,l}|{\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}+\delta{|\hskip-1.084pt|[\sigma_{\alpha,u}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime},l},\mathcal{N}]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}{|\hskip-1.084pt||{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\right)ds

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\omega^{2}}{4}+\frac{\omega\mathcal{Z^{\prime}}\delta}{2}+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta.

(135)

In (1) we have used the bound
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha}\rho(t)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}<\omega/2+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta
derived in the first part of this lemma to insert the bounds
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha,r}^{\dagger}(s)|{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\alpha,l}(s)|{\rho(0)}\rrangle|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq\omega/2+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta
. We have also used the fact that at most
2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
terms
\mathcal{N}_{\beta}
do not commute with
\sigma_{\alpha}^{(u)}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. The lemma statement is confirmed choosing
u\in\{-,+\}
.
∎

Dynamics
. We now establish the counterpart of Lemma
5
in the presence of errors. This requires additionally providing bounds on
\sum_{\beta}\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(t)
,
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(1)}(t)
and
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(2)}(t)
. We begin by providing a counterpart to Lemma
13
.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Then for
q_{\alpha},\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s)
, with
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(2)}
, as defined in Lemma
8
,

{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha,\delta})\right|}\leq 2\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg)\text{ for any }\alpha,

and,

{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}\leq\left(4+\frac{8\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\right)\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg)\text{ for any }\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},j.

Furthermore,

{\left|\textnormal{Tr}\big(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(s)\big)\right|}\leq\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg)\text{ for any }\beta,

and

{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\beta}(s))|}\leq 2\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta},S_{O})+d({S}_{\alpha},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).\text{ for any }\alpha,\beta,j

where
\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}
is the set of system qudits contained in
S^{\prime}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

The bound on
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})|}
is a restatement of the bound given and proven in Lemma
13
. The bounds on
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}
are proven in the same manner as the bounds on
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}
in Lemma
13
.

First we consider
\text{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s))
. For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
we obtain that

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}((\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))]])\right|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega}{\left\|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}((\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}\right){\left\|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]]\right\|},

where, in the last step, we have used Lemma
9
. Next, we can use Lemma
4
together with the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty},{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(\pm)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
to obtain

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(1)}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(s))|}\leq\left(4+\frac{8\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}\right){|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

Next, for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
, we obtain that,

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\alpha,{\alpha^{\prime}}}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)]])\right|},

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]L_{\alpha}]|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{A}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq 4{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\left(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O}))\right),1\right).

Next, we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}})|}
. For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}\left([L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\delta,\omega}(s))]\right))|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}(O(\tau))]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\delta,\omega}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}\right)\sum_{u\in\{+,-\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}(O(\tau))]\right\|},

where, in the last step, we have used Lemma
9
. Next, we use Lemma
4
together with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
and
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
to obtain

{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}\leq\left(4+\frac{8\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}\right){|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

Next we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}_{1}
. For
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u^{\prime})},\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u}^{\prime})}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))]])|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{({u}^{\prime})},[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},O(\tau)]]\right\|}{\left\|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}^{(\bar{u})}\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u}^{\prime})}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))\right\|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{2\omega}+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\right)\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}{\left\|[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{({u}^{\prime})},[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},O(\tau)]]\right\|},

where in the last step we have used Lemma
9
. Furthermore, from Lemma
4
and the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|[L_{\alpha}^{(u)},\cdot]|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 2
it follows that

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))\right|}\leq\left(4+\frac{8\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\right){|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg).

(136)

Similarly, for
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega^{2}}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})(\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(n_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))))\right|},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\omega^{2}}{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}\big(O(\tau)\big)|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(n_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1},

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\right){|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}\big(O(\tau)\big)|\hskip-1.084pt|},

where, again, in the last step, we have used Lemma
9
. Next, we can use Lemma
3
together with the fact that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|(\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}-\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}^{\dagger}})^{\dagger}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{cb,\infty\to\infty}\leq 4
, we obtain that

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2+\frac{4\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{8\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\right){|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{a}d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})\bigg),1\bigg).

(137)

Eqs.
137
and
136
together establish establish a bound on
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}^{(4)}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s))|}
for any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
and is consistent with the lemma statement.

The bound on
{|\text{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(0)}_{\beta}(s))|}
is proven in the main text. Next we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta})|}
. For any
\alpha,\beta
,

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta}(s))|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},\sigma_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s))])|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\hskip-1.084pt|\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\beta}^{\dagger}([L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},O(\tau)]\sigma_{\alpha})|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta},S_{O})+d({S}_{\alpha},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg),

(138)

where
\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\beta}(X)=\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(X\otimes I_{\mathcal{A}})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\beta}
is supported only on the system qudits in
\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta}
.
Finally, we bound
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta})|}
. For any
\alpha,\beta
,

\displaystyle{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(2)}_{\alpha,\beta})|}

\displaystyle\leq{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}\mathcal{N}_{\beta}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)])|}

\displaystyle\leq{|\hskip-1.084pt|\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\beta}^{\dagger}\left([L^{\dagger}_{\alpha},O(\tau)]L_{\alpha}\right)|\hskip-1.084pt|}{|\hskip-1.084pt|\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s)|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{1}{2a}\big(d(\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta},S_{O})+d({S}_{\alpha},S_{O})\big)\bigg),1\bigg),

(139)

producing a bound identical to that for
{|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}^{(1)}_{\alpha,\beta})|}
.
∎

Lemma
10
, repeated
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable on the system qudits with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local target Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Then for
q_{\alpha}
as defined in Lemma
1
, then there are non-decreasing piecewise continuous function
\nu,\nu^{\prime}
such that
\nu(t),\nu^{\prime}(t)\leq O(t^{d})
as
t\to\infty
and for
\omega\leq 2

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})\right|}\leq\nu(\tau)\text{ and }\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s))\right|}\leq\bigg(1+\frac{2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{4\delta\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}{\omega^{2}}\bigg)\nu^{2}(\tau).

where, for
j\in\{3,4\}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
and for
j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(j)}
where
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(s)\right|}\leq\nu^{\prime}(\tau)

\displaystyle\text{ and for }j\in\{1,2\},\ \sum_{\alpha,\beta}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(j)}(s)\right|}\leq(\nu^{\prime}(\tau))^{2},

where
\mathcal{K}^{(0)}_{\beta}
and
\mathcal{K}^{(j)}_{\alpha,\beta}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
are defined in Lemma
8
.

Proof.

We prove this lemma in the same manner as Lemma
5
, using Lemmas
12
and
20
. Consider first,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{|\text{Tr}(O(\tau)q_{\alpha})|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\alpha}\min\bigg(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{a}\bigg),1\bigg),

\displaystyle=2\xi^{(0,1)}_{a,\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq 2\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq 2e\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau),

(140)

where in the last step, we have used the fact that, as per Lemma
12
,
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
is a non-decreasing function of
\lambda
for a fixed
T
.

Similarly, for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}{|\text{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s)|},

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(4+8\omega\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta+16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\right)\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\min\bigg(e\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\bigg(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha^{\prime}},S_{O})}{2a}\bigg),1\bigg),

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(4+8\omega\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta+16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\right)\xi_{2a,e\eta_{S_{O}}}^{(0,2)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq\left(4+8\omega\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta+16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\right)\big(\max(e\eta_{S_{O}},1)\big)^{2}\left(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(4+8\omega\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta+16\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta\right)e^{2}\big(\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\big)^{2}\big(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\big)^{2}.

(141)

From Eqs.
140
and
141
, it follows that choosing
\nu(\tau)=2e\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)
satisfies the lemma statement.

Next we apply Lemmas
20
and
12
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(S_{O},\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta})}{a}\right),1\right)

\displaystyle=\xi^{(0,1)}_{a,\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\leq\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau),

(142)

where in the last step we have used the fact that, as per Lemma
12
,
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
is a non-decreasing function of
\lambda
for fixed
T
.

For
j\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{\left|\textnormal{Tr}(O(\tau)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(j)}(s))\right|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\min\left(\eta_{S_{O}}\exp\left(4e\mathcal{Z}\tau-\frac{d(\tilde{S}^{\prime}_{\beta},S_{O})+d(S_{\alpha},S_{O})}{2a}\right),1\right)

\displaystyle=\xi_{2a,e\eta_{S_{O}}}(4e\mathcal{Z})\leq 2(\max(e\eta_{S_{O}},1))^{2}\left(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{2}(\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1))^{2}\left(\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)\right)^{2}.

(143)

Considering Eqs.
142
and
143
, we may choose
\nu^{\prime}(\tau)=\sqrt{2}e\max(\eta_{S_{O}},1)\nu^{(0)}(2a,4e\mathcal{Z}\tau)
to satisfy the lemma statement.
It can also be noted from the asymptotics of
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
in Lemma
12
that both
\nu(\tau),\nu^{\prime}(\tau)\leq O(\tau^{d})
. Additionally, since
\nu^{(0)}(\lambda,T)
, for a fixed
\lambda
, is a non-decreasing and piecewise continuous function of
T
,
\nu(\tau)
and
\nu^{\prime}(\tau)
are also piecewise continuous non-decreasing functions of
\tau
.
∎

Long-time dynamics or fixed points
. Next, we consider the problem of long-time dynamics or fixed points.

Lemma
11
, repeated
.

Suppose
O
is a local observable with
{|\hskip-1.084pt|O|\hskip-1.084pt|}\leq 1
supported on
S_{O}
, and for
\tau>0
, let
O(\tau)=\exp(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{\dagger}}\tau)(O)
where
\mathcal{L}
is a geometrically local Lindbladian of the form in Eq.
14
. Furthermore, suppose
O
is rapidly mixing with respect to
\mathcal{L}
and satisfies Eq.
20
with
k({|S_{O}|},\gamma)\leq O(\exp(\gamma^{-\kappa}))
. Then for
q_{\alpha}
as defined in Lemma
1
,

\sum_{\alpha}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)q_{\alpha})e^{-2s/\omega^{2}}ds\right|}\leq\omega^{2}\lambda^{(1)}(\gamma),

where
\lambda^{(1)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-d(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
and for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\bigg(1+\frac{2\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}+\frac{4\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega^{2}}\bigg)\lambda^{(2)}(\gamma),

where
\lambda^{(2)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(2d+1)(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
and for
j\in\{3,4\}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
but with
\rho_{\omega}\to\rho_{\omega,\delta}
and for
j\in\{1,2\}
, we define
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(j)}=\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h_{\alpha^{\prime}}}^{(j)}
where
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,h}^{(j)}
is defined in Lemma
1
but with
\rho_{\omega}\to\rho_{\omega,\delta}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\textnormal{Tr}\bigg(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\beta}^{(0)}\bigg(\frac{s}{\omega^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)ds\right|}\leq{\lambda^{\prime}}^{(1)}(\gamma)

where
{\lambda^{\prime}}^{(1)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(d+1)(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
and for
j\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}\big(O(t-s)\mathcal{K}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(j)}(s^{\prime})\big)e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq{\lambda^{\prime}}^{(2)}(\gamma),

where
{\lambda^{\prime}}^{(2)}(\gamma)\leq O(\gamma^{-(2d+1)(\kappa+1)})
as
\gamma\to 0
.

Proof.

We follow the same method as the proof of Lemma
6
. Since
q_{\alpha}
is defined identically in the noisy and noiseless models, the first statement of the lemma is simply a restatement of the bound from Lemma
6
.
Next we note that, for a function
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}=\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\text{Tr}\bigg({O(t-s)\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\bigg(\frac{s^{\prime}}{\omega^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)}e^{-2(s-s^{\prime})/\omega^{2}}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}.

(144)

We now consider the terms involving
Q^{(j)}
for
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
separately — for
j=1
, we have from Lemma
8
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c.}
with
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},X]
,
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})=-\omega^{-1}\textnormal{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and, using Lemma
9
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2+\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta/\omega
. Thus, applying Eq.
144
and Lemma
14
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(1)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\left(1+\frac{2\mathcal{Z^{\prime}}\delta}{\omega}\right)\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(145)

Similarly, for
j=2
, we have from Lemma
8
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c}
where
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L_{\alpha}^{\dagger},L_{\alpha}X]
,
\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[h_{\alpha^{\prime}},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})=-i\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))/2
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2
. Thus, from Eq.
144
and Lemma
14
, we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(2)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(146)

For
j=3
, we have from Lemma
8
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(3)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\sum_{u\in\{-,+\}}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}^{(u)}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)}(\sigma_{\omega}^{(u)}(s^{\prime}))
where
\mathcal{K}^{(u)}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{D}_{L_{\alpha}}
,
\mathcal{J}^{(u)}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(X)=[L_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u)},X]
and
\sigma_{\omega}^{(u)}(s^{\prime})=-i\omega^{-1}\text{Tr}_{\mathcal{A}}(\sigma_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\bar{u})}\rho_{\omega,\delta}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2}))
. We note that
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{K}^{(u)}_{\alpha}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond},{|\hskip-1.084pt|\mathcal{J}^{(u)}_{\alpha^{\prime}}|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{\diamond}\leq 2
and, using Lemma
9
,
{|\hskip-1.084pt|\sigma^{(\bar{u})}_{\omega}(s^{\prime})|\hskip-1.084pt|}_{1}\leq 1/2+\mathcal{Z^{\prime}}\delta/\omega
. Thus, from Lemma
14
and Eq.
144
, we obtain that

\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s/\omega^{2}}\textnormal{Tr}(O(t-s)\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(3)}(s^{\prime}))e^{-2(s/\omega^{2}-s^{\prime})}ds^{\prime}ds\right|}\leq\left(1+2\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\delta}{\omega}\right)\zeta^{(3)}(\gamma).

(147)

For
j=4
, we have from Lemma
8
that
\mathcal{Q}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(4)}(s^{\prime}/\omega^{2})=\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\mathcal{J}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(\sigma_{\omega}^{(u,u^{\prime})}(s^{\prime}))+\textnormal{h.c.}
according to the following definitions: We define
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}(X)=[L^{\dagger}_{\alpha},X]
.
